The balance runs' drivers take `heir` (runCapital, runGrand and the three danger runs, each cached a heir at a time), defaulting to the Steady, so the older runs and their pins are untouched; Track D's title tests that sat at the first bars move to the new ones (tests/v90/d/titles.test.js, pass.test.js, ui.test.js: the Builder's fixtures 2,500 -> 5,000 units, the words 2,000 -> 4,600). — Cost if wrong: none to the game.

### The fix wave

1630. Every number a sentence quotes about the flood, the unit pay and the work gold is the sim's for this city: the Nilometer's note reads `levelHarvest` (prepared) and `heirLoss` (not), the pay lines multiply the data's pay by `heirFactor 'pay'` (one unit's, to a tenth of a gold; the whole bill is still rounded once in `soldiersPayOf`), and the crews' work gold a month multiplies by `heirFactor 'crews'`. — Cost if wrong: a figure a tenth of a gold off on a unit's line; the bill itself is unchanged.
1631. The rows before a first flood say this city's levels: the farm row's range is `heirLoss` over every level (src/ui/s46/river.js passes it to `S46_RIVER_TEXT.farmRow`), and the First flood card's words come from `FIRST_TEXT.first_flood.whyFor(world)` (createUI reads it when a card has one; the plain `why` stays 8.4's). — Cost if wrong: a Bountiful city reads 60% for a Low flood on those two lines until the flood has risen.
1632. Pass the throne writes the ruler row first, and the city's row and the chronicle stamp only once the row is read back as kept; a failed Pass puts the dynasty's name back as it was and the seal undone. — Cost if wrong: a failed Pass leaves a city row and a stamp for a reign that carried on.
1633. The Pass sheet gives `passSheetExtras` the sealed score as `{ total, parts }` (the kept seal's own parts), and draws the side by side above Pass the throne and Not yet, below the score and name parts. The name field's hint lists the allowed characters. — Cost if wrong: the side by side showed today's parts beside a sealed total, or sat under the buttons.
1634. Bring rulers' Merge keeps the device's dynasty (heir and name) when the device already has rulers, and takes the file's dynasty only on a device with none (a new device restoring); in that case the answer says before Merge "Your next city will be heir of the Builder, under the name Ramesses". — Cost if wrong: a restoring device would start as the Steady with no name until the player sets them.
1635. Play this map's choices (level, size, layout, seed) are applied to the New city card at once, and given back when the card is backed out of (Back, Esc, or Back from the replace question); a city starting keeps them. — Cost if wrong: a player who looks at a map and backs out keeps its level and seed for the next New city.
1636. The side by side's line wraps under 480px wide (name and number above, the bar across the full width), so large text never squeezes the bar to a sliver. — Cost if wrong: at 390px and 150% text the bars read as stubs.
1637. The running frame cap steps a due time by a whole period (1000 / 60 ms, 2 ms of slack, resynced when a loop falls a period behind) instead of measuring from the last frame drawn less 4 ms, so 90, 120 and 144 Hz screens all draw close to 60 a second (the gate test holds each within 55-61); the sim still steps every frame, and the idle 30 cap steps the same way. — Cost if wrong: a screen whose frames land oddly against the due times draws a frame more or less than 60 in a second.
1638. Each layer is counted in one budget only: V's effects (the river's flow, the wakes, the weather, the dust, 9.0's workshop smoke, the festival and the rest) in V's dropper, and ambient's own (glints, ripples, sway, rare life, its own smoke) in ambient's, which leaves out whatever V spent while it drew. The weaver's, jeweller's and builders' guild's smoke is timed into V's budget, so ruling 1602's shed order for V stands and ambient's rare, smoke, sway order runs on ambient's own cost. — Cost if wrong: a frame's time is split by measured spans, so a layer timed oddly can count a little to the wrong side.
1639. Small leftovers of V: a ferry's wake is drawn only while the crossing's own boat is (not while a cart's boat has taken its place); the dead #panel fade in css/v90.css is removed (the side panel never toggles hidden; the sheet and the toast keep theirs); a dust puff fades in over the first fifth of its life and a sand gust fades in and out over its window; and V's own Still and Gentle branches (the trade wake's, the river flow's) are removed, effects being Full only (ruling 1601), while the older 8.x motion branches stay as they were. — Cost if wrong: none to speak of; a Reduce motion form would be added back by hand.

### The controller

1640. The heirs' long-run misses are accepted as measured: the scripted cities, tuned to the Steady, do not adapt to a trait (T1 Builder +260 days; T3 every heir loses seed-5 raids, B2 U7 F6 M6 G2, and seed 10 F2 M1 G2; F, M and G miss the great-work win; T2 Builder gold -26%). Heirs come only after a first Pass, hours in. — Cost if wrong: a heir's city is harder than the plain one until a retune.
1641. Josh: no reduced motion (records 1601's reversal of the plan's Q18). — Cost if wrong: see 1601.

## Part 28: Version 9.1 "First hours" (8 Nov 2026)

Josh asked for broad polish after 9.0 and for fewer refunds in a store's first window (about 2 hours of play within 14 days). The design (specs/2026-11-07-thebes-polish-design.md) was approved with every recommended answer except one: no colour-blind work (ruling 1700). The rulings follow in number order within each piece of work, as each wrote them: wave 0 (the shared pieces), Track F (the first hours and the lesson's hand-over), Track R (never stuck: the error card, the loading screen, Back to gold), Track P (runs well), Track A (clear and trusted: About), Track H (the heirs against adaptive scripted cities), the wave 2 balance and targets check, the fix wave, and the controller's own. The files are docs/superpowers/notes/2026-11-07-version-9-1-rulings-{w0,f,r,p,a,h,balance,fix}.md.

### Wave 0, the shared pieces

1650. This release sits before the decree as 9.1 "First hours"; the decree becomes 9.2. — Cost if wrong: renames only.
1651. Wave 0 adds the seams, each a stub with 9.0's behaviour: `createGameLoop({ ..., onError = null })` (given, a throw in a day's tick calls `onError({ where: 'tick', error })` and ends that frame with no more days, no events delivered and no draw, the backlog dropped; a throw in the render calls `onError({ where: 'render', error })`; the loop never stops itself, and without `onError` a throw leaves the frame exactly as on 9.0); `installRescue(ctx)` installs nothing and is called once by `boot`, before the title or a development flag's game opens, with `{ win, doc, root, store, settings, records, io, hooks, game: () => current }`; `createUI`'s `autosave` asks `blocked()` first, which reads a per-interface flag (so per city) that only `ui.setAutosaveBlocked(flag)` sets, false until Track R sets it; a blocked autosave calls no save and raises no save-failing banner (the update's Restart saves through `hooks.save('update')` directly, outside `autosave`, so Track R blocks that one too); `window.__thebes.caches()` returns `renderer.cacheBytes()`, `{ terrainBytes: 0, spriteBytes: 0 }` until Track P fills it in renderer.js (the one renderer the title and every game share), so P never edits boot.js; `isUnlocked` counts a type in `world.lessonCards` as unlocked, after the one-great-temple rule and before rank and prestige; index.html's `<!-- v91 loading -->` and `<!-- v91 early-error -->` blocks (each closed by `<!-- end v91 ... -->`) sit in the head before the first stylesheet and `<!-- v91 preload -->` after css/v91.css, the last stylesheet; text.js's sections F, R, A, P, H in merge order. — Cost if wrong: none to the game; a track that needs a seam shaped differently changes it in its own file and says so.
1652. `world.lessonCards` is optional (absent: none), so there is no world-version bump. The save writes the world whole (`serialize` is `JSON.stringify`) and neither the migration nor `validateWorld` drops a field it does not know, so the list survives a save and a reload with no save change; `validateWorld` does not check its shape and `isUnlocked` ignores anything but an array. — Cost if wrong: one migration later if it becomes required; a hand-edited save with a bad list loads and unlocks nothing extra.
1653. The profile (`tools/profile.ps1`) measures outside the suite; only in-page counters and the lesson's waits are suite tests. — Cost if wrong: a throttled regression is seen only when the profile is run.
1654. A 9.0 lesson city already adopted at Market town is loaded unchanged. — Cost if wrong: a few players keep the old hand-over's rank.

### Track F, first hours: the lesson, the hand-over, the first city

1655. At the hand-over the rank returns to what the people earned (`updateMilestone` from Village: a Village at 94 people), the staged ranks' splits are cleared, the raid clock stops until a real city's 250 people, and the lesson's cards stay unlocked. — Cost if wrong: a player who expected to keep Market town sees Village.
1656. The lesson's firehouse moves so its reach covers the buildings the lesson asks for (ruling 1670 says where and what it leaves). — Cost if wrong: the lesson's day pins move once.
1657. The lesson's staged warning brings its raiders 30 days on (`LESSON_RAID_LEAD`, set on `warning.arrives` by `leadRaid` in the lesson's own city only, idempotent, so the sim's arrival, the countdown and every word agree; the `raid_warning` event keeps the sim's own arrives, which no interface reads). The raid step's speeds are ruling 1671's. — Cost if wrong: the lesson's raid feels quick.
1658. In the lesson the banner carries only the step's own alert: the fire at the fire step, the raid's warning and the raid in the defence chapter, none elsewhere (the lesson's seam names the codes on the root's `data-lesson-alerts`; alerts.js filters the banner by it, never the list or the Map badge). Without the attribute every critical shows, as before. — Cost if wrong: a real problem in the lesson's city waits for the hand-over.
1659. A toast that echoes the player's own action (placed, laid, demolished, undone) shows but is not kept in Messages (`tray: false`). — Cost if wrong: a player looking for "Road laid" in Messages finds nothing.
1660. A device's first own city defaults to the classic river at Normal size while its records hold no city (`firstCityLayout`); Surprise me from then on; a picked layout is kept as before, and a `?variant=` flag still names the map. — Cost if wrong: a first city is plainer than it could be.
1661. `world.lessonCards` holds the cards the lesson put in the player's hands beyond the earned rank: each step's own card and every kind standing in the city, of those the staged Market town opened (in practice the tower). The rest of Town's and Market town's cards are locked again and lose their "New" tags, and a card in `lessonCards` is not called new when its rank arrives (`updateMilestone`). — Cost if wrong: a card the lesson showed but the player never used (the palace) waits for Town.
1662. The hand-over gives the city a fresh city's raid state (no clock, no warning drawn, index 0, adaptation 1), keeping only the report of the raid just beaten, so the city's first real raid is its first (it pauses the game and cannot be paid off). — Cost if wrong: a player meets a "first raid" a second time.
1663. The hand-over writes `world.handOverDay` (optional, like `lessonCards`; no world-version bump). For its first game month the lesson seam marks the root `handover-month`, and tips.js holds a tip (it waits in the slot, as a tool holds it) while an alert has the banner and a goal shows, so no more than two rows stand under the top bar. The banner itself was already one row with "+N more". The picture cards whose moment the lesson itself taught (the Large plot's) are marked seen at the hand-over, so no card opens over the handed-over city (the Large plot's card opened over a 1280x800 hand-over shot, as it has since 8.1). — Cost if wrong: a tip a new player could have used waits up to 30 seconds; a player who skipped through the Large plot step never sees its card.
1664. The road step marks the start road's end with the real game's teal diamond (its colours, a little smaller: ruling 1674) and the 9 tiles to lay with a dashed ghost, drawn as a still SVG above the dimming (after the scrim), hidden under a card and at every other step; its words are "Drag west from the end of the road (the diamond)." — Cost if wrong: one more thing over the map at the first step.
1665. The month step's why is mood's ("Mood falls when homes lack food or work: the mood chip says why."), and it lights and leaves live the mood chip beside the gold. The taxes' line it replaces is said by the gold's own explainer and the step's reaction. — Cost if wrong: a player learns about upkeep from the gold chip rather than from Ipi.
1666. While the lesson runs every message is kept already read (the tray's `readWhen`), since its captions are hidden behind Ipi's row; the hand-over's new interface starts with an empty tray as before. — Cost if wrong: a lesson player who opens Messages sees no count for what is there.
1667. The game's story is told once, on the title card; the lesson's first card says its chapter's why like every other card (ruling 1110's goal line on the first card is dropped). — Cost if wrong: a player who starts the lesson from a development scene never reads the story.
1668. The Housing plot bar's first line with a mouse is "Tap a spot, or drag for a row" (a finger's was already "Drag or tap tiles"). — Cost if wrong: none.
1669. I've played before (on the title) marks the lesson done and starts a city at once on the new-city card's choices (ruling 1660's default), over ruling 1111's title again; New city in the menu offers the rest. — Cost if wrong: a player who wanted Hard for the first city starts it from the menu's New city.
1670. The firehouse stands at (20, 38), the corner where the new road leaves the start road: it covers the granary, the bazaar and every home, and leaves the farm one road tile beyond its reach. No free 1x1 spot but the well's reaches both the farm and the southmost home (15 road tiles apart against a reach of 8). Measured over 18 idle months at the lesson's staged rank: no fire (the design's first candidate (17, 40) left three homes out and caught 7). Accepted by the controller: a farm is low risk and raises no fire alert. — Cost if wrong: the farm can catch fire in a city that grows past Town without a second firehouse.
1671. The raid step plays its wait at 3x until the raiders arrive (`LESSON_RAID_WAIT_SPEED`, the step's `waitSpeed`), then their march and the fight at 2x so it stays watchable (`speed`), then 1x when the step is over (`stepSpeed`); its words say so before Play ("The game runs at triple speed until the raiders arrive, then double."). The lesson changes the speed only from its own: a speed the player picks is kept through the change of phase and at the step's end, and the lesson's own speed goes back to 1x when its interface goes (left, skipped, restarted). P5 measured: the wait 29 days at 3x, 9.7 s; the march and the fight 45 days at 2x, 22.5 s (9.0: 134 s at 1x); the workers step 31 s at 1x, 1 s over the 30 s bar, left (no honest way to trim it: its days are the homes filling, the sim's own); all waits together 97 s, inside 150 s. — Cost if wrong: the lesson's end keeps about 32 seconds of watching the raid come.
1672. The "Good plots" legend row (the house tool's spots) is held while a lesson card is up (`spotsOn` reads the store's `lessonCardUp`, which the card sets as it shows and hides, so the row redraws when the card goes), so it never talks beside Ipi's card. — Cost if wrong: none.
1673. The title's backdrop may draw its showcase at zoom 1.5 on a screen 1600x900 and up (`showcaseFrame`), so at 1920x1080 the sample city fills its band round the card; every smaller screen keeps 8.2's zooms (1, 0.75, 0.5). The card's own size is left to the stylesheet. — Cost if wrong: the showcase's sprites drawn at 1.5 on a big screen.
1674. The road step's view (and its Show me) moves east of the spot's middle by what the diamond at the start road's end needs to be whole, but never so far that the long ring is cut (ruling 821's rule), and the diamond is 0.6 tile each way (the game's guide marker is 0.8): at 375 and 390 px both are whole. — Cost if wrong: on a phone narrower than 375 px the diamond's east corner touches the edge.

### Track R, never stuck: the error card, the loading screen, Back to gold

1675. A throw in a day's tick taints the city: the loop stops within that frame, the game pauses, the card shows, and autosave and the hooks' save are blocked for that world (the update's Restart and Home too, which then wait as for a failed save), so the slot keeps the last month's save and its backup. The card offers Reload from the save (the page reloads, to the title with Continue), Export this city and Copy the details, each with its consequence under it before the tap. — Cost if wrong: a city that was in fact sound is not saved until Reload (at most one game month lost).
1676. The loading screen is built by an inline script in the head (markup in the head would end it early) as soon as the body exists, from the page's own inline CSS, and boot removes it when the title shows or, with a development flag, when its game starts (`rescue.loadingDone`, which also calls `performance.mark('thebes-ready')` once, for Track P's profile). After 15 s it says "Still loading. Slow connection?" with Reload; a `nomodule` line says the browser is too old, with no Reload. — Cost if wrong: none; the screen is one element.
1677. The early error line is a capture-phase `error` listener (a failed script element's error does not bubble) and an `unhandledrejection` listener, active only while the loading screen is up: a module that fails to load, a syntax error or a start that throws is said on the loading screen with Reload. After the title shows it does nothing, and the rescue handler takes over. — Cost if wrong: a failure in a dynamic import after the title shows only the card catches.
1678. A throw in the draw is counted; three frames in a row stop the loop (a frame that drew resets the count, `rescue.frameOk`). The city is sound, so it is saved now and the card shows with Reload. Uncaught errors that arrive ten within a second are one failure that keeps coming (a throw in `onEvents` or the frame gate repeats every frame) and are treated the same way. — Cost if wrong: ten distinct handler errors in a second would stop a healthy game (the card says the city was saved).
1679. A throw in a handler, a timer or a promise gives one toast a session, "That didn't work; your city is safe", and the game goes on; later errors only add to the details. Not failures of the game and so ignored: the browser's ResizeObserver notice, `AbortError` (a cancelled share), `NotAllowedError` (a blocked sound), the cross-origin "Script error." and a rejection with no reason. Before the title, an error is said on the loading screen if it is still up, else the card shows (Reload and Copy only). — Cost if wrong: one of those filtered errors was real and went unseen.
1680. One card a session. It is a sheet at the screen's edge (the bottom on a phone, the bottom corner on a wide screen), Tab stays inside it and Esc does nothing: the way out is Reload. Copy the details is the version, the build stamp, where it threw, the device's user agent and each error's first lines (name, message, three frames), with no address, query or hash; when the clipboard refuses, the text is shown selected. The dev hook `?crash=tick|render|ui` is read once at boot in `rescue.js` and nowhere else: `tick` puts a getter on the world's buildings that throws once, only when `tickDay` reads them (the first day after Play); `render` throws three frames in a row; `ui` throws once from a timer 1.5 s after the game starts. — Cost if wrong: none for a player; the flag is for tests and Josh's real-phone check.
1681. The debt alert's View, wherever it is tapped (the banner, the Messages tray, the alert list, Welcome back), opens Back to gold. Its fix line reads "Pause what costs most; View shows which, and the ways out"; City advice's treasury item reads the same line. — Cost if wrong: that advice item points at a View it does not have when the treasury is not yet below 0.
1682. Back to gold lists this month's taxes, wages, upkeep and soldiers from `monthFigures`, then the three buildings that cost most (upkeep plus its staff's wages as `monthFigures` charges them, never one Pause would be refused or one already paused), each with Pause (`setPaused`, then the labour reassigned). A row says "saves N" only while no level of work is short of workers (`world.labour.shortLevels` empty), because then the workers freed have no job to go to; else "saves up to N: its workers may take other jobs". — Cost if wrong: with workers short the month gains less than the row's cost, and the row said "up to".
1683. The months are this month's balance run forward: months to clear a debt is `ceil(debt / net)` when net is above 0 and none otherwise; the room before `ECONOMY.debtFloor` (-500, where `canAfford` refuses every purchase) is `gold + 500`, and at a falling balance its months are `floor(room / -net)`. Trade, court dues and events are not in them ("at this rate"). — Cost if wrong: a caravan or a court due moves the real month.
1684. The levers are listed only while the sim honours them. The tax lever needs a palace and is one step up from its rate (Low to Normal, Normal to High), worth exactly `taxesAt` of the step less today's taxes, with the mood it costs (-8). The sale lever needs an open route whose caravan or boat will land (`arrivalAt`) and is the best household good or luxury a partner buys (never food, which the homes eat, nor raw goods, which workshops use), above the homes' use for three months rounded up to the Trade sheet's step of 10 and what the temple and court are owed, up to the caravan's quarter of the yearly quota, worth at least 10 gold; it says "Set X to Sell with Keep at least N", and with that order the next caravan pays exactly the gold shown. Each has a Show me (the palace's sheet; the Trade sheet). — Cost if wrong: a surplus the player would rather keep is offered; the Trade sheet's own Keep at least is the control.

### Track P, runs well: memory, the profile, the load

1685. No bundler: the module preload block is generated by `tools/sw-files.ps1` (every module src/main.js imports through static import and export-from lines, depth first, main.js first; a dynamic `import()` stays lazy) and kept only if P1 improves. — Cost if wrong: a slow first load stays slow until a bundle is chosen.
1686. The caches budget by bytes: terrain chunks by a canvas byte budget (48 MB, fixed whatever the ratio: at DPR 1 it holds about 21 chunks at zoom 1, still under the count of 32), sprite buckets dropped when unused, never in view. — Cost if wrong: more re-bakes when a player zooms back and forth.
1687. A chunk the frame shows is never evicted for the budget, at any scale, and the terrain gives way to the sprites: each frame its budget is the lesser of 48 MB and 64 MB less the sprites held, never under 16 MB (so a sprite-heavy view keeps its ring rather than letting it go and baking it again), set as the frame begins and again after the sprites are drawn, so the two keep target P3's 64 MB together; a view that itself needs more (a hilly view at zoom 2 at DPR 2, where every hill is its own sprite slot, about 0.8 MB each) holds only what it shows. The ring's pre-bake bakes ahead only into room the budget has once chunks the frame has not looked at are let go. — Cost if wrong: zooming back to a part of the map left a while ago re-bakes a few chunks; the worst hilly view holds about 77 MB.
1688. The profile (tools/profile.ps1) is headless Edge at 390x844, DPR 2, through tools/profile-serve.py (gzip as the real host, HTTP/1.0, no-store, so a warm load can only use the service worker); the ready point is the page's `thebes-ready` mark when set, otherwise the title card appearing (the table says which); a frame's cost is the drawn frame's whole requestAnimationFrame callback (the days ticked and the draw), and memory is the JS heap after a forced collection, `window.__thebes.caches()` and a census of every live canvas. Headless Edge draws canvas without a GPU and the machine runs other suites, so the frame figures bound the obvious, not a phone. — Cost if wrong: a figure off by the machine's load; Josh's real-phone pass is the check.
1689. The sprite cache drops a zoom bucket left for 30 s whole, never the bucket in use, and keeps 16 MB by the sprites least recently drawn, never one this frame draws (a cache used without frames evicts nothing). The renderer warms a figure's walk poses again after a bucket is dropped, not after a single sprite is evicted (that pose is drawn again when next needed, so the budget never makes the warm-up loop). — Cost if wrong: a figure's turn at a long-unused zoom draws its pose then, a millisecond or two.
1690. The profile's session is compressed: the seed-11 sample town on a Large map, its days ticked by the sim's own `tickDay` with the events handed to the interface as the loop does, a frame drawn by the interface's own `render` every 10 days, and the camera over every chunk at every zoom bucket after each 10 minutes of play (three game years), not each game year, so 2 hours of play fit the profile's 10 minutes on this machine. — Cost if wrong: the session pans less often than a restless player; the suite's P3 test pans the whole map at every bucket.
1691. Frames (P2, P4) are measured and reported, with the biggest costs from the profile's CPU sampling (`-Layers`); no frame code is changed in this release (design 5d). — Cost if wrong: a slow phone stays slow until a frame track takes the biggest cost.
1692. The module preload stays (ruling 1685's test): the throttled cold load's ready point was 17.2 and 17.5 s with it against 17.8 and 18.0 s without it on the same tree (and 18.1 to 18.8 s on 9.0), about 3% better. The profile's server speaks HTTP/1.0, so the browser fetches six at a time and the preload can only fill the gaps; on the real host's HTTP/2 every preloaded module is asked at once. — Cost if wrong: 377 link lines in index.html (about 2 KB gzipped) for half a second.
1693. The warm load (P1's 1.5 s) is not measured: in headless Edge here the service worker's install ends redundant, though the same page caching the same list with `addAll` succeeds; it goes on Josh's real-phone list (install, then launch offline) with the question whether the offline cache installs at all. — Cost if wrong: a warm load or offline start that does not work is found on a phone, not here.

### Track A, clear and trusted: About

1700. No colour-blind work in 9.1: Josh dropped it (8 Nov 2026), so Track A makes no colour test, pattern, hue or layer change and is About and the network test only. — Cost if wrong: the colour-blind check the first design asked for stays undone.
1701. Options gains a closed About section (a `details` whose 44 px summary is the heading, Enter or Space opens it) above the version and build at the foot, and the About sheet shows the same four lines: "Made by Josh", "Licence: <licence>", "Art, music and sounds are made by the game itself.", and the privacy line. The licence words are one string, `V91_A_TEXT.aboutLicence`, "All rights reserved", the licence Josh chose. — Cost if wrong: one string to change.
1702. The version and build in About are never typed: the line is `versionLine`'s, from `APP_VERSION` (the newest release's version, which a test pins) and the build stamp read off sw-files.js; Options keeps its own foot line (ruling of 8.0) and the About sheet now shows the build too. — Cost if wrong: none.
1703. About is folded in Options so the sheet stays short on a phone; a test opens Options on 375x667 and 390x844 at 100, 125 and 150% text with About open and checks the sheet budget's line for a sheet (rows end by 20% or 25%, the alert row only), the 44 px summary, every line inside the frame and no sideways scroll. — Cost if wrong: the sheet scrolls a little further.
1704. The privacy line is held by a test: every file under src/ (read from sw-files.js's list) is scanned with comments removed, and the test fails on XMLHttpRequest, a beacon, a socket, an event source, importScripts, a dynamic import of a web address, a `fetch(` call outside the tutorial's read of its marks (the build stamp's read goes through a passed-in fetch, and a second test pins its address as sw-files.js beside the page), or a web address in code other than the XML namespaces and the share link's base. — Cost if wrong: a future same-origin fetch in another file fails the test and is added to the list with a reason.

### Track H, the heirs against adaptive scripted cities

1710. The scripted players learn a habit per heir's weakness, reading the sheets a player reads; the Steady's runs are unchanged. The Steady has no habit, so a run with `adapt` and the Steady is the 9.0 run to the last number (a test compares the capital run's year-6 world, serialized, with and without it: Capital on day 600, 50,457 gold, lowest 255, score 360). — Cost if wrong: the scripts play heirs better than a new player does.
1711. The heirs are retuned by `TRAITS` data only, where an adaptive run still misses T1-T3. — Cost if wrong: a trait felt less than designed.
1712. The court's yearly due and the Nilometer's line read the heir. `outputPerMonth` (the court's reckoning of a month's output: request and gift sizes, the bumper harvest, an old save's first quota) is times production.js `heirRate`, so the Bountiful's farms count 15% more and the Merchant's workshops 15% less, as they make them; the Nilometer's sheet and its first say this city's Low flood, "40% becomes 60%" for the Bountiful (heirLoss, the sim's rule) and 9.0's "60% becomes 73%" for every other heir. How to play keeps the static line: it states the game's rule, not a city's. — Cost if wrong: none; the words match the sim.
1713. `outputPerMonth` does not read the flood. Reading it would change the size of every city's requests, gifts and bumper harvests, the Steady's too, which this release may not do (no sim rule changes but the lesson's and `TRAITS`) and which would move every Steady balance pin. The yearly quota already reads what the city really made (the year's snapshot), flood and heir included. — Cost if wrong: in a Low-flood year the court asks a farm town for a little more grain than its farms made that month.
1714. The Builder's habit (taxes x0.95): once the great temple is founded, the palace's High rate while the treasury holds under a month of Normal-rate taxes, back to Normal at two months; a palace is laid, on the free spot farthest from any home that no script claims, when there is none and the treasury holds it with 400 to spare. Measured first: from day 0 below three months, High at the capital run's day 600 changed the whole run and the great work was not won by year 20; from Capital below one month, the same; gated on the founding, the great-work run plays as 9.0's until year 16 and wins on day 6,235 (the Steady's 6,380: T1 met), never in debt (lowest 713). — Cost if wrong: a Builder player who never touches taxes still finishes 260 days late.
1715. The Unconquered's habit (first warning 100 days sooner): every warned raid is met with towers by the building each band strikes first (the late run's `meetWarning`), from the capital years on. With it the seed-5 dangers run beats all 13 raids (9.0: lost 6-12), seed 10 all 11, the late run all 10 and the late dangers run all 10 (T3 met); the great work is won on day 6,245 (T1 met, -135) and the year-6 gold is 49,794 (-1.3%, T2 met). No data change: `sooner` stays 100 (ruling 1626's 100 -> 15 is not needed). — Cost if wrong: an Unconquered player who builds towers only by the script's count still loses the seed-5 raids.
1716. The Merchant's habit (workshops x0.85): it keeps round(n / 0.85) of each workshop kind where the scripts laid n (a fourth potter by the three), each on the nearest free spot within 8 tiles of one of them that a road touches, no script claims, and has its input and a store for its goods in reach (else taken back, refunded), with the gold (400 to spare) and the workers to staff it. — Cost if wrong: the Merchant's runs still lose to a shortage the trait's line names.
1717. The Bountiful and the Generous get no habit. Their weaknesses never bite in the balance runs: no balance map has a floodplain (the capital map has no water, so a flood never touches its farms) and no script sets the High rate. Their runs differ from the Steady's only by their strengths, which move the thin-margin scripts (rulings 791, 1543, 1627). — Cost if wrong: their T1 and T3 misses stay unexplained by a habit.
1718. `adapt` is opt-in, off by default, so every older balance run and pin (the Steady's and 9.0's T4-T7 heir measures) plays as it did; tests/v90/balance/runs.test.js passes `adapt` and its T1 and T3 pins are re-set, each marked WAVE2-PIN. The habits never build on ground a script will want (the capital script's steps, the great-work run's temple plot and streets), so every script step still goes where it went. — Cost if wrong: none to the game.
1719. No `TRAITS` change in this release. The Builder's T2 (year-6 gold 37,278, -26.1%, NOT MET by 1.1 points: its habit waits for the great temple) was tried with taxes 0.95 -> 0.96: T2 met (-19.8%) but the great work moved to day 6,777 (T1 missed) and seed 10 lost 4 raids, so it is not kept. The Merchant's, the Bountiful's and the Generous's misses come from the thin-margin scripts, where a trait's number moves the result by chance (ruling 1625's taxes 0.93); a number that met them would be a pin, not a balance. — Cost if wrong: the Builder keeps a quarter less gold at year 6 than the Steady, and three heirs lose seed-5 raids the Steady beats.
1720. The heirs' table (target P8), 9.0's runs reproduced first with the scripts as they were (every number as ruling 1627's), then with the habits. T1, the great-work run (Steady day 6,380, band 6,180-6,580): the Builder 6,640 -> 6,235 (met), the Unconquered 5,958 -> 6,245 (met), the Merchant not won -> 6,938 (by day 7,200, 358 past the band), the Bountiful and the Generous not won (no habit). T2, the capital run (Steady day 600, 50,457 gold, lowest 255): every heir reaches Capital on day 600 but the Generous (510); year-6 gold the Builder 37,278 (-26.1%, NOT MET by 1.1 points, unchanged), the Unconquered 49,963 -> 49,794, the Merchant 49,184 -> 54,255, the Bountiful 50,457, the Generous 52,944; none below 0. T3, raids lost that the Steady beats: seed 5 the Builder 2 -> 2, the Unconquered 7 -> 0, the Bountiful 6 -> 6, the Merchant 6 -> 2, the Generous 2 -> 2; seed 10 the Unconquered 0, the Builder 0, the Bountiful 2, the Merchant 1 -> 2, the Generous 2; the late, late dangers and grand dangers runs lose none for any heir. Met in full: the Unconquered (T1-T3) and the Steady; the Builder T1, and T3 on seed 10 and the late runs, but not on seed 5 (its habit waits for a great temple that run never founds), nor T2's gold. — Cost if wrong: a heir that loses a long dangers run in play is caught only by players.

### The balance and targets check

1725. The balance check owns every wave-2 pin and the lesson's day pins. — Cost if wrong: none.
1726. The lesson's workers step waits 31 s at 1x, 1 s over P5's 30 s bar, and is accepted (controller): its days are the homes filling under the sim's own rules, which the lesson may not hurry. — Cost if wrong: a new player watches homes fill for half a minute.
1727. The lesson's farm stands one road tile beyond the firehouse's reach and is accepted (controller, ruling 1670): a farm is low risk and raises no fire alert, and no free spot reaches both the farm and the southmost home. — Cost if wrong: the farm can catch fire in a city that grows past Town without a second firehouse.
1728. The lesson's day pins (9.0's T7) are re-pinned: tests/v84/r/lesson.test.js DAYS_83, tests/v90/balance/heirs.test.js DAYS_84 and tests/v91/f/lesson.test.js DAYS_91 'raid:65>199' -> 'raid:65>139', and RAID_END 198 -> 138. They moved because the staged warning now brings its raiders LESSON_RAID_LEAD (30) days on (ruling 1657): day 93, not 153. Every other step keeps 8.3's day (the firehouse's move, ruling 1670, moves none); all 14 steps are reached with every heir on the device, the raid is won and its step done. — Cost if wrong: none; each comment keeps the old value.
1729. P5's pin is re-pinned: the raid step's wait 134 s at 1x -> 32.2 s (29 days at 3x, 9.7 s, then the march and fight, 45 days at 2x, 22.5 s), all waits together 97.2 s (inside 150 s). Read as one wait the raid step is 2.2 s over the 30 s bar; ruling 1671 counts its wait and its fight as two, each inside 30, and that reading is kept. — Cost if wrong: the lesson's end keeps about half a minute of watching the raid come.
1730. Track P's two P3 pins in tests/v91/p/caches.test.js are kept as set (new in 9.1, design target 64 MB): the two caches over 64 MB in fewer than one frame in ten, and never 80 MB (the worst, a hilly view at zoom 2 at DPR 2, held 77 MB: ruling 1687). The profile's two-hour session agrees: 62.8 MB at its end, 74.7 MB at its peak. — Cost if wrong: a phone that drops canvases at 75 MB finds it in that one view.
1731. Track H's heir-run pins in tests/v90/balance/runs.test.js are kept as set, each moved by the heir's habit (`adapt`, rulings 1714, 1715), not a rule: the Builder's great work won on day 6,640 -> 6,235 and its lowest gold -3,204 -> 713 (T1 met); the Unconquered's capital run raids beaten and lost [3, 0] -> [4, 0], its seed-5 raids lost [6-12] -> none, its lowest gold there -28,072 -> -19,997 (T3 met). All green on the merged branch (bal91-0). — Cost if wrong: none; each comment keeps the old value.
1732. Every 9.0 frame bound holds after the merge; no bound was moved. Merged against 9.0, read in turn: the stress frame 4.1-4.8 / 17.8-20.7 ms at zoom 1 / 0.5 (9.0 here 3.5-4.2 / 17.1-18.7, ruling 1628 2.8 / 17.1; bounds 50 / 120), V on against off -0.5 to +0.4 / -0.6 to +1.2 ms (T8: +2 / +4); V's layers 0.2 (0.5-0.7 flushed) at zoom 1 and 0.4 (0.7) at the floor (T9: 2 / 4, flushed 14); three live sites 0.31-0.43 ms (T10: 1); seasons and light 0.6-1.0 ms (3.4-4.5 flushed) (T11: 2, 14); the battle layers 0.47-0.54 ms, the frame over peace +0.5-1.4 near and +4.0-5.7 far (T11: 3; 6, 15). Two merged readings taken just after the full suite, while the machine was still busy, were up to twice these (the stress frame at 0.5 28-40 ms, the far battle 41-49 ms) and the untouched light and supply-line layers doubled with them; read in turn with the 9.0 tree they match it. — Cost if wrong: a phone's frame is not a headless desktop's; the real-phone pass stands.
1733. The targets on the merged branch (390x844, DPR 2, headless Edge, no suite running), each miss with its size, its biggest cost and the change that would come nearest: P1 cold (slow 4G, CPU 4x, 4 runs): first paint 968 ms (met; 9.0 820-948 ms); ready 16.6 s (9.0 18.1-18.9 s), MISSED by 11.6 s. Network alone (CPU 1x) is 14.2 s: 387 requests and 1,627 KB, about 8 s of bytes at 1.6 Mbps and the rest round trips over HTTP/1.0's six connections; CPU alone (network open) is 6.7 s, of which building the title's sample city is about 1 s (168-258 ms unthrottled). Warm: the service worker installed in headless Edge in two of five tries (ruling 1693 saw none); its second visit was ready in 4.9 s throttled and 7.2 s on CPU alone, MISSED by 3.4 s, all CPU. An HTTP-cached second visit never reached the ready point within 60 s and is not measured. A bundle would not meet P1: the JS is 1,456 KB gzipped as 378 files, 1,268 KB as one file and about 770 KB with whole-line comments stripped, so a bundle cuts about 375 requests and 0.2-0.7 MB, roughly 16.6 s to 8-10 s, still over 5.0 s while the boot alone is 6.7 s at 4x. Nearest change: smaller JS (the comments are half its gzipped bytes) and a cheaper boot, then a bundle if the real host's HTTP/2 figures still need it; no bundle in 9.1, since on its own it would not meet the target. P2 (the stress city, 4x): p50 58.9 ms, p95 104.7 ms (6x: 78.6 / 140.7; 9.0 as measured by Track P, under load: 117.7 / 126.7), MISSED 3.5x / 3.2x. Biggest costs: drawImage 35% and the raster outside script 48% (headless Edge draws canvas without a GPU). Nearest change: the map at DPR 1 (27.3 / 46.7 ms, still 1.6x / 1.4x over); no single change measured meets it. P3: caches 62.8 MB at the end (peak 74.7 MB, ruling 1730), the heap 17.4 MB, its second hour +3.6%: MET (9.0: caches unbounded, 455 MB of live canvases; heap 16.9 MB, +3.7%). P4 (Huge, 4x): zoom 1 p50 81.6 ms, worst 113.6 ms; the zoom floor p50 31.9 ms, worst 58.9 ms (9.0: 161.2 / 524.8 and 88.0 / 220.8). The median at zoom 1 MISSED by 2.5x; no frame over 250 ms (MET). Biggest cost: setTransform 24% and the raster outside script 59%. The map at DPR 1 meets it (21.2 ms p50, worst 51.6). P5: ruling 1729 (raid step 32.2 s, workers 31 s, 97.2 s together; 9.0 134 s). P6: a Village at 94 people, Town at 150 next (1.6x): MET (9.0 Market town). P7: no own action counted, at most 5, at most two rows, no fire risk on a lesson building: MET (9.0 five messages in month 1, four rows). P8: ruling 1734. P9: dropped. P10: the loop stops within the frame, the card shows, a tainted slot is never written, the save loads: MET. P11: the suite 4,893 passed, every frame bound held (ruling 1732), every pin re-pinned here, mobileBudget at both sizes and three text sizes: MET. — Cost if wrong: headless Edge is not a phone; the real-phone pass stands, and P1, P2 and P4 are decided on it.
1734. The heirs (target P8): Track H's table stands (ruling 1720) and no `TRAITS` number changes. The misses left, each accepted with its size as ruling 1640 did: the Builder's year-6 gold 37,278, -26.1% against the 25% band (by 1.1 points), and two seed-5 raids lost (11, 12); the Merchant's great work on day 6,938, 358 days past the band, and two raids lost on each seed (seed 5: 11, 12; seed 10: 3, 8); the Bountiful's great work not won and six seed-5 raids lost (6-11) and two on seed 10 (3, 5); the Generous's great work not won (one raid lost) and two raids lost on each seed (seed 5: 11, 12; seed 10: 4, 8), its Capital on day 510 on the 90-day bound. One number within the design's bounds was tried for each that has one: the Builder's taxes 0.95 -> 0.96 (Track H: T2 met, the great work moves to day 6,777 and seed 10 loses 4); the Merchant's workshops 0.85 -> 0.90 (both seeds' raids all beaten, but the great work is no longer won) and 0.95 (not won, seed 10 loses 3); the Generous's arrivals 1.2 -> 1.15 (not won with four raids lost, seed 5 still loses two). The Bountiful's weakness and the Generous's never bite in the balance runs (no floodplain, no script sets High) and the strengths stay 15% (design Q5), so no number reaches their misses. Every number tried breaks another target, the thin-margin scripts' chance (rulings 791, 1543, 1627). — Cost if wrong: a heir that loses a long dangers run in play is caught only by players.

### The fix wave

1735. The city the lesson's hand-over adopts is not one the player started: its records id is kept in the setting `lessonCityId`, and `firstCityLayout` leaves that row out when it counts, so a lesson player's first own city (and one after "I've played before") opens New city on the classic river, and the next on Surprise me, as ruling 1660 says. — Cost if wrong: a device that adopted the lesson's city and never started another keeps one extra setting; nothing else reads it.
1736. A lesson step that sets a speed of its own (the raid's) says it on its card before Play, as the docked row does: "The game runs at triple speed until the raiders arrive, then double." — Cost if wrong: the raid card is one sentence longer.
1737. Goals the city already meets at the hand-over complete quietly (`settleGoals` without events, in `adoptLesson`): no toast, no message, nothing counted; their gold is paid as on any completion. — Cost if wrong: the hand-over's first goal pays its gold without a cheer.
1738. The error card closes any open picture cards (`hooks.closeCards`, without their onDone, which may start a city) so it is the one panel, and says only what is true of the save: "Your city was last saved this month." and Reload "Goes back to the last save", since a save also happens when the tab is hidden. The dead strings `limitHead` and `where.storm` are removed. — Cost if wrong: a player who had picture cards open loses their place in them when an error shows.
1739. Back to gold lists buildings of one kind as one row with a count ("2 Builders' guilds: 152 a month"); Pause stops every building in the row and takes the whole cost off the month. — Cost if wrong: a player cannot pause one of two like buildings from this sheet (the building's own sheet still can).

### The controller

1740. No bundled build in 9.1 (Josh, 8 Nov 2026: "No, ship without"): P1 misses at 16.6 s, and one bundle would give an estimated 8-10 s while the boot alone is 6.7 s at 4x; smaller JS and a cheaper boot come first. — Cost if wrong: a slow first load on a phone until the next release.

## Part 29: Version 9.2 "A city you can read" (10 Nov 2026)

The two design reviews of 9.1 (8 Nov) came before new content, so the weekly decree moves to 9.3. The design is specs/2026-11-08-thebes-version-9-2-design.md; the full text of each ruling is in notes/2026-11-08-version-9-2-rulings-<track>.md (w0, l, q, s, d, a, w, v, balance, fl, fa, fw). One line each here, in number order; gaps say "unused".

1750. Wave 0 adds seams only (the roll-up shape, the court's gate, score v3's slot, the text sections, the rigs); the suite passes as on 9.1. — Cost if wrong: a seam that changes behaviour moves a 9.1 pin before any track owns it.
1751. No world-version bump (WORLD_VERSION stays 12): every new world field is optional and absent means today's behaviour. — Cost if wrong: old saves refuse to open or a migration is needed late.
1752. SCORE_VERSION 3 is flipped by Track S with the formula, not by wave 0. — Cost if wrong: a best is labelled "older scoring" while its formula is unchanged.
1753. The six-year Capital fixture is built once per suite run from `runCapital(72, { dangers: true })`; tests that change it take a fresh copy. — Cost if wrong: a test that changes the shared world leaks into the next.
1754. D13 moves from Track L to Track D (the Pass's files, `passSheet.js` and `boot.js` `startHeir`). — Cost if wrong: Tracks L and D both edit `boot.js` and merge badly.
1755. The end card (the city's numbers, one line per thing learned, Keep building this city, Start my own city) ends both lesson paths; it waits for a sheet, the confirm or a step card, and Back closes it with the row's What next? to bring it back. — Cost if wrong: a player who closes it by Back needs one tap more.
1756. The month step stores its entry month on the lesson world (`lessonMonth0`, never saved) and counts two months from it; its wait grows by 30 days. — Cost if wrong: a longer wait (the lesson waits test is re-pinned).
1757. In the lesson a road drag begins at the end of the road (a start further off is refused in the bar); in every city a piece of road that joins no road, edge or building gets the roadless plots' amber outline, a badge and a toast. — Cost if wrong: a free player who lays roads ahead of plots sees amber until the first building touches one.
1758. Services rank ahead of safety until Town (`priorityOrder`); from Town 9.1's order. — Cost if wrong: a Village's firehouse is staffed later; balance scenes with a firehouse in a Village move.
1759. At the hand-over the tips about what the lesson placed or met are marked seen (`LESSON_TAUGHT_TIPS`). — Cost if wrong: a player who skipped a part never sees that tip (How to play has it).
1760. Swift Town is judged on day 300 (was 720); the scripted city becomes a Town on day 180. — Cost if wrong: a plain good start misses a feat it used to earn at 690 days.
1761. The raid step's frame follows the band, pulls out to hold tower, target and band, and on a phone puts the band first leaning to the tower; the scrim has two holes and the tower wears a ring. — Cost if wrong: the camera moves a few times in a 25-second step.
1762. The fire step plays at 1x until the camera has settled on the blaze, then returns to the player's speed. — Cost if wrong: none (a second or two at 1x).
1763. Esc on a lesson card means Leave (with its question); Enter and Space stay Go. — Cost if wrong: a player who presses Esc to dock the card gets a question.
1764. The lesson row is the bold instruction and its count; Ipi's why is behind More. — Cost if wrong: one more control in the row.
1765. Chapter 2's card says "About four in ten people here will work." (from `ECONOMY.workerShare`). — Cost if wrong: none.
1766. After the hand-over the tips wait until the goal in view is done and 600 game days have passed, never beyond 1,800 days. — Cost if wrong: tips come ten to thirty minutes later in a first city.
1767. A short workplace's alert names who took its workers: the kind of building holding the most workers on the level just ahead of its own. — Cost if wrong: a wrong building named when two levels both compete.
1768. The lone-road predicate lives in `src/sim/loneRoad.js` (memoised, never saved); a road reaching the map's edge counts as joined. — Cost if wrong: a road to the edge that goes nowhere is not marked.
1769. The homes step stays at six plots (only seven fit on the road's west side). — Cost if wrong: the lesson city runs a little short of workers in chapters 4 and 5.
1770. Nine plots dropped (controller): D1's labour order already removes the bazaar's shortage they were for. — Cost if wrong: the same short workers in chapters 4 and 5.
1771. The post-hand-over tip hold's 1,800-day cap is accepted. — Cost if wrong: a player who ignores the goal for half an hour gets tips again.
1772. The month step plays at 2x and says "at double speed", so its wait is 19.5 s at the walk-through's pace. — Cost if wrong: a player who changes speed during the step waits longer; the step still ends.
1773. Unused.
1774. Unused.
1775. Alerts roll up by cause (`rollUpAlerts`, one row per code with its count and ids); the flat per-building list stays for markers and advice; letters and one-off notices never merge. — Cost if wrong: two different reasons under one code read as one problem, and View steps through the lot.
1776. The Map badge counts rows; the Minimap sheet opens with City advice's top three under Fix these first, then the rows; View on a rolled row steps through its buildings. — Cost if wrong: a phone player with one row per fire sees one badge number less than before.
1777. One gate over the four letter streams (`letterGate`, optional): one letter at a time, 5 to 8 months apart, the stream served longest ago first, each open letter holding the gate for at most 6 months. — Cost if wrong: an old city gets its first letter on the old schedule and the gate from then on.
1778. The goals strip and rank bar move into the side panel above the minimap on the wide layout while the panel is unfolded. — Cost if wrong: a folded panel on a wide screen shows the strip over the map again.
1779. The far zoom for markers is 0.6 and under: one badge per rolled row on its frontmost building, one for homes about to lose a tier, no footprint rings. — Cost if wrong: at 0.6 a city shows a count where it showed single badges.
1780. The ring's white halo is lighter at every zoom. — Cost if wrong: a ring is harder to see on pale ground.
1781. Unused (reserved).
1782. Unused (reserved).
1783. Unused (reserved).
1784. Unused (reserved).
1785. Unused (reserved).
1786. Unused (reserved).
1787. Unused (reserved).
1788. Unused (reserved).
1789. Unused (reserved).
1790. Unused (reserved).
1791. Screenshot mode moves into Options > Display; Share score stays. — Cost if wrong: a player who knew the menu entry looks in Options.
1792. Interface tidies: one badge to a tray card, the card shows the price alone, toasts are parchment with an ink outline, Options is Sound, Display, Controls, Help, Saves, About, New city's replace note is amber. — Cost if wrong: a test or a habit that read the upkeep line on a card.
1793. The debt sheet's closed levers say why, with Show me only where there is something to show. — Cost if wrong: a reason the sim cannot tell apart reads as the nearer one.
1794. Toasts never stack: a toast of a group never shows beside another of it, and one that waits shows once when there is room (the install ask waits). — Cost if wrong: the install ask on a phone shows later than its 12 s window suggests.
1795. Score v3: a term that has not started counts nothing and is out of its part's ceiling; growth runs to the Seat; Monuments divide by every kind's prestige (1,280). — Cost if wrong: the score reads lower than v2 for the same city (Capital 338 against 360), so a Bronze is harder.
1796. Medals 240, 320 and 400 of 500 (v2: 200, 300, 400; v1 and v2 keep theirs by version). — Cost if wrong: Gold at 400 is out of reach of every scripted run (best 353).
1797. SCORE_VERSION 3, the share link writes `t3`; `t2` and `t1` open as "older scoring". — Cost if wrong: a friend on 9.1 cannot open a `t3` link ("newer format").
1798. Villa tax 6 to 7. — Cost if wrong: Villas pay 17% more, so a late treasury is a little easier.
1799. Peaceful's defence never starts (ceiling 460 raw), so its medals are no longer free. — Cost if wrong: Peaceful's Gold needs 87% of a lower ceiling.
1800. Superseded by 1810: the middle monuments' shorter durations (small mastaba 72, colossus 42 crew-months) were undone. — Cost if wrong: none (superseded).
1801. Great City goal "Open a route that sells a luxury" (`luxury_route`), met by `world.tally.luxuryBought`. — Cost if wrong: a route with no Buy order leaves it open (the why line says to set one).
1802. The Unconquered's weakness: every raid warning 30 days sooner (9.0: the first only); changed to 15 by 1858. — Cost if wrong: later raids come sooner for the heir for the whole reign.
1803. The Generous's lasting cost: taxes x0.95 at every rate; changed to x0.98 by 1859. — Cost if wrong: a lower tax for the whole reign.
1804. The Builder's bar 4,600 to 8,000 monument units. — Cost if wrong: the Builder is rare; no scripted run earns it.
1805. The Merchant's bar 0.40 to 0.25. — Cost if wrong: a city that trades modestly now earns it.
1806. Peaceful's words say what still comes, rarer ("No raids, sickness or thefts. Low Niles, locusts and fires still come, rarer."), from `HAZARD_LEVELS`. — Cost if wrong: none (words).
1807. A monument site whose sheet has not been opened for a month gets one caption, once until it is opened again. — Cost if wrong: a site left alone on purpose gets one reminder a month after each look.
1808. The health "crowded" term is dropped from the daily risk (it was true of every home of every tier); the plague keeps its own use. — Cost if wrong: grand homes catch illness at 0.15 a day where they caught 0.45, so late illness is rarer.
1809. The score chip's explainer shows each part against its ceiling and one line for each term not started. — Cost if wrong: none.
1810. (Controller.) The middle monuments keep 9.1's durations (108 and 48 crew-months): shortening them cascaded through every scripted run. — Cost if wrong: the design's D7 is not answered; a later track can shorten one at a time.
1811. The two v70 "Keep at least keeps the great temple's offerings" tests test something again (the great-work run is won as in 9.1). — Cost if wrong: none.
1812. The score does not fall when a term starts; the scripted Town-to-Market-town dip is the Culture part's staffing flicker, and a test checks the score with the clock never under the score without it. — Cost if wrong: none for the formula.
1813. The City summary's part bars draw each part against its own ceiling under v3; a score with no ceilings is drawn against 100. — Cost if wrong: none (display only).
1814. Review of the diff: the plague's rules keep `crowdedPerTile`, and the trait, difficulty, explainer and luxury-goal words were checked against the sim. — Cost if wrong: none.
1815. The sealed-ruler card is a sheet kind `passSealed` between the Pass sheet and the heir card; nothing is sealed until the heir card's Start. — Cost if wrong: one extra tap before the heir's city.
1816. An heir's city starts with no beginner's guide, on the sealed city's layout (Surprise me when it is not offered). — Cost if wrong: a returning player who wants the guide has Resume guide in How to play.
1817. The heir's first screen has one voice (Ipi's line); the "is remembered as" toast is gone and the install ask waits for the first month end. — Cost if wrong: the ask lands a month later than before.
1818. A Rulers row is headed by the cartouche on its own line, drawn once (`cartouche.js`); a name of 16 characters or more is `long`. — Cost if wrong: a 15-character line may still be tight at 150%.
1819. The title's record line is "Best reign" with the score and the medal of the best's own version. — Cost if wrong: a player with several bests cannot tell them apart by line.
1820. The install ask appears once, at the top of the Rulers list. — Cost if wrong: none (the heir's toast is held, not removed).
1821. Unused.
1822. Unused.
1823. Unused.
1824. Unused.
1825. The confirm dialog is a real modal: Cancel takes focus once, Tab cycles, closing returns focus to the opener, `z-index: 100`. — Cost if wrong: a sheet that rebuilds the opener leaves focus on the body (fixed by 1875).
1826. A sheet opened from the keyboard takes focus at its title and makes the chrome behind it inert; a sheet opened by a tap or click does not take focus in. — Cost if wrong: a mouse player using a screen reader gets no focus move on a click-opened sheet.
1827. Words return at large text for the Layers legend, Goods supply, the tip's label and the lesson's label; the Ground legend's words and the cheer's why stay hidden. — Cost if wrong: Josh may want words under the top-bar pictures or the Ground title back.
1828. A fourth text step, 200%, with its own mobile budget (70% of the height on a 667 px phone). — Cost if wrong: landscape phones at 200% are not budgeted.
1829. Pinch zoom is allowed (the viewport no longer forbids it; the map canvas cancels iOS gestures). — Cost if wrong: an accidental two-finger touch on the bars can zoom the page.
1830. Teal text is #1A5F57 and `TEXT_PAIRS` grows from 14 to 28, all at 4.5:1; small sizes scale with the text setting. — Cost if wrong: coral and amber words on parchment-3 are not listed.
1831. The minimap canvas is a control (role, name, tabindex), and the arrow keys move the view by a sixteenth of the city. — Cost if wrong: some screen readers pass keys through.
1832. The alerts' live region is two: `#live` polite and `#live-urgent` assertive. — Cost if wrong: a rig without the second element gets one made beside the first.
1833. Screenshot mode keeps a small pinned Show interface button; `color-scheme` is light. — Cost if wrong: the button is in the picture of a screenshot.
1834. The compact rail names each icon in a 12 px word inside the 56 px rail. — Cost if wrong: "Monuments" may clip a pixel or two on a wider font.
1835. One name per thing, by the glossary in the Track W note (the lesson, Skip the lesson, City advice, the title screen, Layers, Options, Keep stocked, plot, tile, Great City, map number, pleasantness, Yr 1 Mo 3). — Cost if wrong: a name a player prefers is one string away.
1836. A What's new heading prints the version and the date only; the build stays in Options and About. — Cost if wrong: none for a player.
1837. The error card's "Where:" line is in the copied details only. — Cost if wrong: a bug report from a screenshot lacks it.
1838. The short date is "Yr 1 Mo 3". — Cost if wrong: two tokens longer than "Y1 M3".
1839. Map number, pleasantness and the prestige lock line, as in the glossary. — Cost if wrong: players who knew "seed" find "map number" once.
1840. Figures typed into sentences are read from the data, each with a test that sets the data both ways. — Cost if wrong: a text getter that throws on a missing id.
1841. T13: no player string in the words files is over 90 characters outside listed exemptions, and a second test fails if an exemption covers nothing. — Cost if wrong: a long function-built row can still slip in.
1842. "Skip the lesson" replaces "I've played before"; older release notes keep the old label, and the docs step rewrites the newest. — Cost if wrong: a returning player learns a new label for a button they used twice.
1843. Release notes before 9.1 are edited in place for the same words and cut where a sentence is no longer true or cannot be felt. — Cost if wrong: none.
1844. How to play sentences a player cannot act on were cut; the leave-the-lesson confirm is one sentence. — Cost if wrong: none.
1845. Track V is cut first if the weekly limit nears 90% before wave 3. — Cost if wrong: none (built).
1846. Roads get a kerb shadow and a darker cart track, baked into the terrain chunk. — Cost if wrong: roads a shade busier at near zoom.
1847. At the far zoom an unbuilt plot or monument site is a sand square with a shadow, not halo, string and stakes. — Cost if wrong: a far plot reads less like "pegged out".
1848. Burnt rubble is a soot patch, a charred wall stump and seven blackened bricks. — Cost if wrong: the stump is hidden where a front neighbour stands over it.
1849. The Health layer draws a home's footprint edge solid when a staffed physician reaches it and dashed when none does; the ring and dot go. — Cost if wrong: a busier map in a big city.
1850. Fit the city: `cityBounds`, `fitCamera` and `frameCity` zoom to every building, never in past zoom 1; the Map button's double tap and a key frame the city. — Cost if wrong: a double tap flickers the Minimap sheet shut and open.
1851. The key is F (and Shift+F); Ctrl+F stays the browser's. — Cost if wrong: one line in `KEYS` moves it.
1852. A city whose built area does not show at the opening zoom opens framed. — Cost if wrong: a very big city opens at the least zoom.
1853. The score chip's icon is a medal. — Cost if wrong: none.
1854. Cartouche headings wrap, balanced over two lines (superseding an ellipsis). — Cost if wrong: a taller heading on a very narrow row.
1855. Track L's labour order moves no long run; the Steady pins that moved came from Track Q's court gate and were re-pinned as measured (superseded in part by 1866). — Cost if wrong: the long scripts are thin-margin, and Josh may want more favour per letter.
1856. Superseded by 1866: the Steady's great-work run goes to -6,413 under Q and S together. — Cost if wrong: a careful player at the Seat can owe the court for a month or two.
1857. A bug in the great-work script (the temple's footprint was never cleared), fixed in `greatWorkRun.js`. — Cost if wrong: none to the game.
1858. The Unconquered's warning is 15 days sooner at every warning (was 30). — Cost if wrong: the weakness is half what the design said.
1859. The Generous's taxes are x0.98 at every rate (was x0.95). — Cost if wrong: a 2% weakness is small; High's 1.15 is still the felt part.
1860. Superseded by 1866: the careless city's dues. — Cost if wrong: none.
1861. Numbers superseded by 1867: the Builder, no number changed. — Cost if wrong: the Builder's great work is late in the scripted run.
1862. Numbers superseded by 1867: the Merchant, unchanged. — Cost if wrong: as 9.1's.
1863. Numbers superseded by 1867: the Bountiful, unchanged. — Cost if wrong: as 9.1's.
1864. The medals (240, 320, 400) hold on the merged code, month by month on the scripted capital run; no scripted run reaches Gold. — Cost if wrong: Gold stays out of reach of a scripted Capital (best 341).
1865. (Controller.) Each request and demand is worth twice the favour, won or lost (`REQUESTS` and `DEMANDS` doubled). — Cost if wrong: one letter now moves favour 20 to 40 points, so the court's mood swings in bigger steps.
1866. The Steady's runs re-pinned with 1865: the great work won on day 6,388 with 250 the lowest, B3 1.64, the late run's 2.54 years of profit. — Cost if wrong: the great-work run's lowest is thin (250).
1867. The heirs re-measured with 1865: the Unconquered meets every target, the others as listed in the balance note. — Cost if wrong: the Builder's T1, met on 9.1, is missed.
1868. The trait numbers re-tried and kept; the six-year Capital lists 13 alert rows (T5's bound is 12). — Cost if wrong: T5 is missed by one row on a city that never answers its letters.
1869. The medals re-measured with 1865: the great-work run's sealed score is 352 (Silver). — Cost if wrong: none.
1870. The lesson's raid comes in by its own entry on the south edge, (26, 47), so tower, target and band share a frame on a 375 px screen. — Cost if wrong: the raid always comes from the south and has less road to walk before the strike.
1871. "Start my own city" opens the title's New city card on the classic river. — Cost if wrong: the front card's lesson note is not seen on that path.
1872. The end card's order is the numbers line, the two choices over their hints, then the lines learned. — Cost if wrong: a player may act before reading what they learned.
1873. The row's "n of m" chip sits before the step's words inside the clamped body. — Cost if wrong: the count reads between the label and the words.
1874. A lost raid's line is "N people live here, with G gold. The raid got through; staffed towers stop it." — Cost if wrong: it no longer says "in the treasury".
1875. A confirm opened from a sheet gives focus back to its opener's live successor after the sheet is rebuilt. — Cost if wrong: two rows with the same class and words give focus to the first.
1876. The Minimap sheet's actions are +, - and Fit the city (key F) in both layouts; How to play names the button. — Cost if wrong: a third button in a row that wrapped at 200% text.
1877. On the wide layout the Minimap sheet draws no minimap of its own, and the side panel keeps at least one card row and scrolls. — Cost if wrong: at 150% text the panel scrolls a little where it was clipped.
1878. Screenshot mode focuses Show interface on entering and the Menu button on leaving. — Cost if wrong: a mouse user sees a focus ring only if the browser treats the focus as keyboard focus.
1879. `createDialog` returns `destroy`, and Options' sections are labelled groups. — Cost if wrong: a screen reader's heading list still lacks the sections.
1880. The Rulers rows wrap a long ruler name, balanced over two lines (superseding the ellipsis of 1818). — Cost if wrong: a row with a very long name is one line taller.
1881. Several monument sites due their month caption at one month end get one caption naming the count. — Cost if wrong: the count says nothing of how far each is built.
1882. The Health legend's two keys draw a 2 px edge, solid and dashed. — Cost if wrong: the swatch is a little heavier than the map's edge.
1883. Ipi's bubble says the Generous heir as "Heir of the Generous. 20% more newcomers; taxes 2% lower." and drops "of the harvest" from the Bountiful's. — Cost if wrong: the bubble leaves out that High taxes take 1.15x.
1884. The New city card's Peaceful line reads "No raids, sickness or thefts; rarer fires and Low Niles; buildings 20% cheaper." — Cost if wrong: locusts, also rarer, are left to the hint's full text.

## Part 30: Version 9.3 "A city that grows, and the decree" (10 Nov 2026)

Two halves. *A city that grows* (specs/2026-11-10-thebes-version-9-3-a-city-that-grows-design.md) changes the look of homes, ground, streets and services as the city ranks up. *The weekly decree* (specs/2026-11-10-thebes-version-9-3-the-weekly-decree-design.md) is a six-year run on the week's map with nine decrees; the roadmap's "Pharaoh's levy" is **The Vizier's Levy** in the game, because "Pharaoh" never appears in game text. The full text of each ruling is in notes/2026-11-10-version-9-3-rulings-<track>.md (w0, h, b, g, n, m, p, d0, r, s, u, k, then fix-a, fix-xu, fix-xb, fix-xh). One line each here, in number order; gaps say "unused". Where a later ruling supersedes an earlier one the line says so, and the earlier line stays as it was written.

1885. The eight ranks fold into four looks (Village, Town, City, Capital); a new look spreads from the palace (else the built-up middle) and reaches the farthest building 120 days (`REBUILD_DAYS`, 4 months) after the rank-up; a rank with no recorded day counts as rebuilt. — Cost if wrong: one constant and the test that names it.
1886. A building keeps the day it was placed (`b.day`, optional, so old saves load); one placed after its rank-up starts in the current look. — Cost if wrong: a test that deep-compares a fresh building moves by `day` alone.
1887. Slot keys run variant, look, state (`house.t3.v1.l2`, `bazaar.s1.l2.shut`); a type that needs no workers is `work` unless paused. — Cost if wrong: the key order is an interface every track names.
1888. `MATERIALS[look]` gives wall, roof, cornice, trim, band, ground and paving from the palette's own triples; the Capital's wall is the honey limestone so it reads apart from the City's whitewash; `silhouette` is added to the primitives. — Cost if wrong: a colour swap in one table.
1889. The look sheet is its own page (`looksheet.html`) and its reference art lives in `src/dev/looksheetArt.js`, so the tracks do not collide. Amended after Josh's review of the sheet: colour stays in every look (the bazaar's four awnings), the granary keeps today's shape (four domes on a walled base) in every look, a shut granary reads shut, and the three Tier 3 variants differ in massing (wide block with a canopy; stepped house with a stair; wind-catcher and court). — Cost if wrong: the tracks copy from one file; the amendments are the bar the later fixes are held to.
1890. `HOUSE_LOOKS` is exported unexpanded and registry.js runs it through `withLooks(..., { variants: 3 })` (a drawing file cannot call it as it loads). — Cost if wrong: none, one place expands the tables.
1891. Homes are `house.t{1..9}.v{0..2}.l{0..3}`; `house.lot` stays one slot; the plain `house.t<n>` aliases `.v0.l0` for the card thumbnail. — Cost if wrong: a card shows the Village hut; six older tests now expect `.v0.l0`.
1892. Variants differ in massing, not detail (Josh's note): v0 the tier's own block, v1 stepped, v2 a courtyard; the tier's roof tell stays on every variant. — Cost if wrong: only the drawings change.
1893. The far drawing is `house.t{n}.l{look}.far`, chosen at camera zoom 0.6 or under (`FAR_ZOOM`): the v0 shape with no clutter. — Cost if wrong: one constant.
1894. One height and one shadow set per tier, whatever the variant or look. Superseded for tiers 6 to 9 by 2011 (the heights stay one per tier). — Cost if wrong: a ground shadow a few px off on a variant that is not v0.
1895. A City or Capital big home's walls are 0.7 px taller than a Village one's, so no look is shorter than the Village look. — Cost if wrong: a sub-pixel height.
1896. The sick cloth reads the door from the drawing (`homeDoor(tier, variant)`), the fixed table kept as the fallback. — Cost if wrong: the cloth hangs where the door was.
1897. The 2x2 homes keep their courtyard front-left and the pool at one place in all variants, so the ambient glint still sits on the water (changed by 2010 only where the plan moves a roof, not the pool). — Cost if wrong: the glint floats; change `BIG_POOL` and ambient together.
1898. Today's drawings stay as `HOUSE_TODAY` (and H1..H9) for the look sheet's "today" column and the older pixel tests. — Cost if wrong: dead code to delete later.
1899. Colour stays in every look: painted doors, awnings, canopies and the tier's roof tells keep their colours; the look changes walls, roofs, trims and bands. — Cost if wrong: none to the sim.
1900. Every non-landmark service and workshop runs today's drawing through one recolouring pen (`styled()`) with the look's materials, a band, a pot, lamp or palm, a lit doorway when working or short and a plank door when shut. — Cost if wrong: Josh may want each drawn by hand; the pen is the one part to replace.
1901. The bazaar, granary and potter's kiln are lifted from the look sheet, with the fill stages (granary 0-3, bazaar 0-2) kept in every look. — Cost if wrong: the stage cues are redrawn to the reference's style.
1902. Old keys stay (`granary.s2`, `potter`, `quarry.idle` and the rest); `<type>.idle` is an alias of the Town look shut. — Cost if wrong: about two hundred more slot keys, drawn lazily.
1903. The eight landmarks change by additive shape (granary, bazaar, temple, kiln, brickworks, weaver, well, firehouse). The granary part is retired by 2007: its drums and porch are gone. — Cost if wrong: the extras drop one at a time.
1904. A workshop short of workers shows no goods in its yard; a shut one also shows no figure and no smoke, and its flag hangs lowered; a short storehouse still shows its stock. — Cost if wrong: a short storehouse looks fuller than a short brewery.
1905. The art files export `*_LOOKS` tables and registry.js expands them at its end. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1906. `serviceState` reads `b.blocked === 'idle'`: a quarry or brickworks no monument site needs employs nobody, so it is shut. — Cost if wrong: one clause to revert.
1907. The quay and the ferry landing take the states too, the wharf's stone unchanged; the firehouse's, tower's and barracks' mud faces take the look's wall. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1908. The art draws no smoke itself; the animated smoke is `ambient.js`, which already reads `isWorking` and `b.blocked`. — Cost if wrong: a short building keeps smoking in ambient, as today.
1909. The lifted drawings leave out `groundShadow`; the renderer draws each slot's shadows. — Cost if wrong: it doubled the shadow and went outside the bounds.
1910. Ground edges have sixteen points a diamond and a finer octave of seeded noise, so a straight edge leaves its ideal line within every tile. — Cost if wrong: ragged edges (turn `EDGE_DETAIL` down) or longer bakes.
1911. Edge details (tufts, wet-sand flecks, crumbs, pebbles) and a stepped river bank with a damp line, drawn in batches. — Cost if wrong: marks read as noise at far zoom.
1912. Every building but farms, walls, gates, bridges, rubble, plazas, gardens, statues and sphinxes has a trodden yard 0.3 tile past its footprint in the look's ground; near buildings of one look share a patch. — Cost if wrong: a yard under a type that should stand on bare ground.
1913. A worn path runs from a footprint side to the nearest road when one or two free tiles lie between. — Cost if wrong: a path crossing a yard it should not.
1914. `chunkSignature` gains each building tile's footprint-edge bits and look, and each road tile's look, only above the Village look; the two-tile ring stays. — Cost if wrong: a chunk re-bakes a frame late at a ring edge.
1915. A road's look is its tile's: dirt track (Village), gravel (Town), stone kerbs (City), paving with joints and a gold centre line (Capital). — Cost if wrong: a road changes look a tile before its neighbour.
1916. A City road is paved only where a building stands within a tile; a Capital road is always paved. The Capital half is superseded by 2012. — Cost if wrong: a long empty avenue stays gravel.
1917. From the City look, a built-up straight road tile with a free verge carries a street tree every third tile (City) or a tree and a lamp alternating (Capital). — Cost if wrong: a tree drawn under a later sprite.
1918. A building's walls have two soft contact-shadow layers at the base, one path a layer for the whole chunk. — Cost if wrong: a shadow under a sprite that draws its own.
1919. Bake time measured on the stress city in the City look: about half as long again (Normal 108 to 171 ms for 16 chunks, Large 218 to 329 ms for 25), mostly the yards; the two-bakes-a-frame limit stays. — Cost if wrong: two bakes in a frame take about 1.5 times as long on a slow phone.
1920. The palace's five styles are redrawn through the primitives; shapes, heights and bounds unchanged. — Cost if wrong: busier at 0.5 zoom.
1921. Trees, rocks, reeds and scrub share one `contactShadow`; palms, acacias, rocks and reeds gain shading. — Cost if wrong: cached tree sprites carry a few more draw calls.
1922. Finished monuments shade their bodies; sites rising course by course are not shaded (they draw live each frame). — Cost if wrong: a finished monument costs 6 to 20 more gradients a frame.
1923. Plaza open edges gain a base line, a lit lip and a faint shade; sphinx plinths take the wall shade. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1924. Every figure and animal stands in `footShadow`, and citizens, women, priests and soldiers gain a shaded side; silhouettes and walk poses are unchanged. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1925. The nilometer, hunting lodge, gazelle, ostrich, croc, fishing boat, archery range and stables take the wall shade or foot shadow. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1926. Rubble and burnt or wrecked shells gain a cast shadow and a lit crest. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1927. Sheaves take a cast shadow; the flood pool a darker near rim. — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1928. The trade boat, ferry and fishing boat share one hull treatment (shaded strake, lit gunwale, dark water line). — Cost if wrong: none for play.
1929. Carts gain a contact patch and a shaded underside. Not done: the raider chariot, elephant and raider boat shadows, the ibis, heron and hippo shadows, the fishing wharf and the charred stump. — Cost if wrong: those rarely seen drawings sit a step below the rest.
1930. A look change raises poles for one game day only on a building drawn within the last game day; no dust puff. — Cost if wrong: a fast pan right after a front passes may miss a pole.
1931. The sweep is one gold ring from the look's centre to the farthest building (at least 6 tiles, plus 2) over 3 s. — Cost if wrong: on a zoomed-in view it leaves the screen in about a second.
1932. Only Full motion sweeps, and only for a rank that brings a new look (Town, City, Capital). — Cost if wrong: a Gentle player gets the caption but no ring.
1933. The sweep is shed before the 9.0 dropper sheds anything: not drawn while the effects' running average is over a quarter of their 2 ms budget. — Cost if wrong: a slow phone never sees the ring.
1934. The rank-up caption's why is "The city rebuilds in the new style over the next months." for a rank that brings a new look. — Cost if wrong: wording only.
1935. The caches are measured on the stress city and the 112 map, in the Capital look and mid-rebuild, at DPR 1 and 2, desktop and phone, over 600 frames of a moving camera. — Cost if wrong: a busier scene evicts more than measured.
1936. Cache budgets by device: a desktop 32 MB of sprites and 80 MB with terrain, a phone 24 MB and 72 MB (9.1: 16 and 64 for all); a desktop is `deviceMemory` 8, a phone less, and where it reports nothing a phone when the screen's short side is under 768 px; P3 is now 72 MB with a worst view of 88 (marked WAVE2-PIN). — Cost if wrong: a phone's tab reloads on a Capital mid-rebuild, or an iPad (no `deviceMemory`, 768 px and over) gets the desktop's budgets.
