1937. What a frame needs is still held, whatever the budget; evictions stay under 0.5% of draws in every scene. — Cost if wrong: about 130 MB of canvas on the 112 map's far view at DPR 2.
1938. The bar's contract (`shadeWall`, `contactLine`, `insetOpening`) is counted in `art/primitives.js` and checked for every registry slot, except figures, fields, palms, hills, rubble, the empty lot and the open works. — Cost if wrong: an exempt type gains a wall and is never checked.
1939. Track B's drawings that called none of the three were listed as owed (every shut state, the plain card keys, the trading post, Quay, brickworks, gem mine, the wall and gate cards). Paid off by 2005 to 2009; the list is empty. — Cost if wrong: shut workshops read a step below the bar.
1940. The slot is answered from caches: `lookOfBuilding` keeps each building's look in a WeakMap per world, dropped when the rank, the day, the splits or the buildings version moves; `slotFor` takes its look, variant, state and far names from tables built once. — Cost if wrong: a look a frame late after a rank day.
1941. No other per-frame allocation in the 9.3 path: `collectObjects` shares its options objects and the scaffold watch skips trees and walkers; scaffolds, the sweep and ambient allocate only while they show. — Cost if wrong: none seen.
1942. The proof and the measure: `slotForFresh` keeps the old way and a test holds `slotFor` to it over the stress city and a Capital mid-rebuild across six months; the 112 map is measured against 9.2 on a quiet machine. Result (600 frames, moving camera, lower of three, p50/p95 ms, 9.2 against 9.3): Capital desktop DPR 1 17.7/112 against 25.0/118; phone DPR 2 25.7/100 against 27.7/112; mid-rebuild desktop DPR 1 18.4/101 against 23.7/115; mid-rebuild phone 26.2/97 against 29.0/108; DPR 2 desktop level or faster. A still camera costs 0.5 ms more than 9.2; the rest is new sprites drawn as the camera moves, with more slots a building. Shipped so, with a follow-up. — Cost if wrong: a slower large city on old phones.
1943. The rebuild front's centre and reach come from the buildings standing on the rank day, so a building placed or demolished later moves no other home's look (review M1). — Cost if wrong: a city whose centre moves a long way after the rank day rebuilds around its old centre.
1944. A touch device that reports no `deviceMemory` (Safari: every iPhone and iPad) gets the phone's budgets (review P1). — Cost if wrong: a touch-screen Safari desktop gets the smaller caches.
1945-1949. Unused.
1950. The plan's week and rotation values were checked against the real calendar and hold. — Cost if wrong: the week's decree differs from the plan's table.
1951. `weekIndex` counts from 2026-W47 and `isPairWeek` is every fourth index (3 mod 4). — Cost if wrong: pair weeks shift by a week.
1952. `ruleFactor` is `heirFactor` times the decrees' factors; every other rule function gives the old number with no decree. — Cost if wrong: the main game moves, and the golden test fails.
1953. `createWorld({ decree })` drops unknown ids, forces the Steady heir and Normal, and needs no change to `serialize.js`. — Cost if wrong: a decree save would not load.
1954. The golden fingerprint (72 month ends of the v92 Capital run, 89 tally keys) is checked in as `tests/v93/d0/golden.json`, proven to bite. — Cost if wrong: a sim change outside the score, gold, people, raids and tally slips past.
1955. `ruleFactor` replaces `heirFactor` in six sim files; a unit's pay is `ruleUnitPay` per kind, summed then rounded once. — Cost if wrong: archers' pay is off by a rounding gold a month.
1956. `capacityOf(world, type)` is the one capacity function and every place that read `BUILDINGS.x.capacity` reads it. — Cost if wrong: a place shows a granary's old 600.
1957. The flood step moves the drawn level along FLOOD_LEVELS and clamps; a drought's forced Low is returned first. — Cost if wrong: Low Nile is a step weaker in a drought year.
1958. The first warning is the decree's gap, less the heir's sooner; the band size is multiplied only from the second raid; the boat share replaces the boat row's base and max. — Cost if wrong: Sea Peoples' first raid is as it was.
1959. Elephants come from `ruleElephantsFrom(world, 4)`, read in `elephantRaid` only. — Cost if wrong: none for other kinds.
1960. `sickSpread` and `fireStart` multiply the data chances at their one call each; the firehouse reach is `ruleReach`. — Cost if wrong: Track U's count and danger sheet still said 8 until they switched (1981).
1961. `courtAsk` multiplies every ordinary request and `courtReward` multiplies the reward on top, so a Levy request paid about four times a plain one. Superseded by 1992. — Cost if wrong: the Levy pays 2x rather than 4x.
1962. `typeReason` and `previewWallTiles` refuse forbidden types with `'decree'`; 28 commands begin with a decree-ended refusal; `runMonth` sets `ended` and speed 0 at the first month end on or after `endDay`. Three commands missed are added by 1993. — Cost if wrong: a command added later works after the end.
1963. `wellMonths` and `fireSafeMonths` are in `newTally` but not `TALLY_KEYS`, so an old save loads and reads 0; they count from Town. A well month is refined by 1991. — Cost if wrong: the bars would want a different start month.
1964. The medal is judged on the sum of the six parts (a decree run is Normal); a pair outside `DECREE_PAIRS` has no medal. — Cost if wrong: a Hard decree run would need a factor.
1965. The save store takes a slot option; `decree` prefixes IndexedDB and localStorage keys, the empty slot is the old store untouched. — Cost if wrong: a decree save under the main keys would overwrite the city.
1966. Records version 3 adds `decrees`; a v2 blob reads with none. — Cost if wrong: an older game reading v3 drops `decrees`.
1967. A decree row's id is `decree:<week>`; one row a week. — Cost if wrong: a duplicate row in the ruler list.
1968. `noteDecree` takes the score from its caller (R owns `decreeScore`); without a score, a decree world or six good parts it returns null. — Cost if wrong: U must pass the score.
1969. The link is `#d1.<week>.<six parts>.<seven splits>.<check>`; an unknown `d<n>` above 1 reads `newer_format`, anything else bad `malformed`. — Cost if wrong: a newer link reads as broken.
1970. A decree rival is a rival row with `week`, one per name and week, kept out of the main game's lists. The words "classic layout" are stale: it carries the week's real map (`decreeMapOf`). — Cost if wrong: a friend's decree score beside a city on the same seed.
1971. `addRival` keeps a decree rival when the link has a good `week`. — Cost if wrong: a decree link read as a main one.
1972. Merging decrees by week keeps the higher score and adds the tries. — Cost if wrong: re-importing your own export doubles `tries`.
1973. Opening a decree link only adds a rival. — Cost if wrong: none.
1974. `decreeShareText` takes its words from `DECREE_SHARE_TEXT`, the sixth part labelled Decree. — Cost if wrong: wording only.
1975. The decree's words live in `U93_TEXT`; "Decree" is the mode's one name. — Cost if wrong: a string change in one section.
1976. A decree run is the lesson's shape: no Pass the throne, heir picker, new city, Export, Home, ruler list or records, saves to the decree slot, no guide and no daily goals. — Cost if wrong: a city row or stamp from a decree run in the records.
1977. The title card sits after the main buttons; the gate is a city row with a Town split or any ruler; a `d1` link opens the card on its week early and keeps a named sender as a rival. — Cost if wrong: a new device sees the card early on a friend's link.
1978. The chip is the first row under the top bar, not in it, because the phone's bar is full at 150% text. — Cost if wrong: one row less map at the top.
1979. The first screen is the decree card over a paused game; Begin starts the clock at 1x; from the chip it says Close. — Cost if wrong: one extra tap on Continue.
1980. A forbidden card stays in the tray, locked, with "Not under this decree". — Cost if wrong: a greyed card in Defence under Open city.
1981. The numbers shown are the numbers the sim uses (`ruleFactor`, `ruleUnitPay`, `ruleReach`, `capacityOf`, the decree's medals); the granary's fill drawing still read plain capacity until 2003. — Cost if wrong: a number on screen differs from the sim's.
1982. The ruler list's decree rows merge with rulers by date: mark, medal, score of 600, six parts, tries, the map; Play this map starts a plain city. — Cost if wrong: a player expects a decree run from the row.
1983. The end card is the score sheet's own parts (`scoreTotalLine`, `scorePartRow`), with Share, Try again and Back to your city above the six parts; the run is noted once (`world.decree.noted`, optional). — Cost if wrong: a second open counts a second try.
1984. R and S are merged and read directly; U's own share line is gone for S's. — Cost if wrong: a try counted without a row.
1985. `runCapital` gains `decree`; the balance file loads only for `-Grep "v93 k balance"`. — Cost if wrong: nobody reruns the numbers after a sim change.
1986. Each bar is the run's measure x 1.25 to two significant figures, and each medal set is the run's total rounded down to 10, +60, +120; Levy's run answers the court and the Host's meets warnings with towers. Several bars and medals are changed by 1990 to 1994. — Cost if wrong: a bar set from a player the decree does not assume is too easy or hard for the Levy and the Host.
1987. The Southern Host's `elephantsFrom` is 1, not 2, because a six-year run never reaches raid stage 1. — Cost if wrong: elephants come a raid earlier than the twist line says.
1988. Medals follow the plain script's last-day total, which declines in its last year (drought from month 61, mood 14), so bronzes range from 210 (Plague) to 410 (the Host). Left as measured; the question of script or game stays open. — Cost if wrong: a bronze on Plague is far easier than on the Host.
1989. Sea Peoples' boats come in a decree run (`boatsFrom` 1); the twist reads "more of them by boat". Four weeks in the first rotation are Great oasis weeks with no river. Josh then ruled (10 Nov) that Sea Peoples never falls on the Great oasis (`decreeMapOf`). — Cost if wrong: about one Sea Peoples week in four played without boats, which the card said.
1990. A months-measure bar is at most 60 (`MONTHS_BAR_MAX`); Fire season's bar is 60 (was 83), its medals [310, 370, 430]. Supersedes 1986 for Fire season. — Cost if wrong: a quick Town could count past 66.
1991. A well month counts only while the city has 15 or more Cottage-or-better homes; Plague's measure is 4 and its bar 5 (was 9). Supersedes 1986 for Plague; its medals stand. — Cost if wrong: a small city can still avoid sickness, and the bonus asks for a bigger one.
1992. The Vizier's Levy has no `courtReward` factor: the ask is doubled and the reward follows it, so "pays double" is true. Supersedes 1961. Stone famine + Levy medals [250, 310, 370]. — Cost if wrong: a Levy week pays less gold than before.
1993. `meetDemand`, `layCrossing` and `sealReign` refuse with `decree_ended`; a test scans the commands the interface imports. Completes 1962. — Cost if wrong: the scan reads source text.
1994. Sea Peoples + Fire season medals [410, 470, 530]. Supersedes the pair in 1986. — Cost if wrong: none.
1995. The week helpers live in `src/data/week.js` (`src/ui/week.js` re-exports them). — Cost if wrong: a stale import path still works.
1996. The top bar's date holds the last day of year 6 once the decree has ended. — Cost if wrong: a player expects the bar to run on.
1997. A decree run has no chapter goals in the strip, the sheet, the City page or the nudge. — Cost if wrong: a goal caption could still fire from the sim.
1998. In a decree run the score chip and the City page read `decreeScore` (of 600) and count to `decreeMedals`. Supersedes the city medals in the summary. — Cost if wrong: the chip's number differs from the city score seen before.
1999. The decree chip has a dot between year and mark; the title card's twist lines are small and left-aligned, its go button plain. — Cost if wrong: looks only.
2000. A finished run is noted when it arrives (`decree_end`), not when the card draws; the row's title is the heir title. — Cost if wrong: a second note is harmless.
2001. After the end the refusal reads "The decree has ended", Play, the speeds and the keys open the end card, and the alerts, tip and scribe are hidden. — Cost if wrong: a debt alert after the end is not seen.
2002. The title's Rulers button counts decree rows; the end card's side-by-side lists six parts; a pair week shows each decree's own part under the bonus; "Week N" keeps its no-break space. — Cost if wrong: the Rulers number counts two kinds of row.
2003. Under Low Nile the granary drawing reads `capacityOf`, completing 1981. — Cost if wrong: a granary shows a step lower than it did.
2004. The elephants' twist reads "War elephants may come from the 2nd raid"; the bonus line lowercases only its first letter. — Cost if wrong: wording only.
2005. Every Track B drawing that never called the bar now does, and the owed list is empty; the plain card keys take their entry from `<base>.l1.work` (the cards show the Town look working). Pays 1939. — Cost if wrong: the plain keys point back at the old draws and the owed list returns.
2006. A shut workshop reads by shape: a barred door at least 0.24 of the face wide and closed shutters (`shutFront`). — Cost if wrong: three numbers in one function.
2007. The granary keeps its shape in every look: no drums or porch at City and Capital; heights [22, 22, 22, 26]. Retires the granary part of 1903. — Cost if wrong: Josh's yes to 1903 restores the drums.
2008. `dress` shades and grounds the body of a shut building; a drawing with no body names its walls in `cfg.bar`. — Cost if wrong: the shade can tint things in front of a wall by a few percent.
2009. The wall and gate cards add the contact line where the run meets the ground (no shade); the map's walls are unchanged. — Cost if wrong: only the card pictures call the bar.
2010. The 2x2 homes (tiers 6 to 9) get distinct plans: v0 an L, v1 a low block with a back tower, v2 a narrow tall block with a set-back storey. — Cost if wrong: a roof tell on the wrong roof.
2011. Each 2x2 variant has its own shadow blocks from the same plan. Supersedes 1894 for tiers 6 to 9. — Cost if wrong: none for other tables.
2012. A Capital road is paved only where it is built up, like a City road, and kerbed elsewhere. Supersedes 1916's Capital half. — Cost if wrong: a Capital's outer roads look like City kerbs.
2013. The Capital street reads differently at play zoom: warm sandstone cream with a gold-brown kerb, three lanes of slabs, palms and planters beside the lamps. — Cost if wrong: only the baked streets change.
2014. Home heights are unchanged by the new plans. — Cost if wrong: none.

Open at the end of 9.3: see notes/2026-11-10-version-9-3-followups.md (the real-phone list, the Capital mid-rebuild on a phone and an iPad, and the script's last-year decline, ruling 1988).

### 9.3.1 "Easier on a phone" (11 Nov 2026): rulings 2015 to 2040

A patch on 9.3. The mobile review (notes/2026-11-11-mobile-ux-review.md: 3 must, 15 should, 14 could) had every must and should item fixed or ruled, and Josh asked for three more: the title logo stays put, a placing building's outline lies under the houses in front, and a bridge or ferry is one straight line. Group notes: notes/2026-11-11-version-9-3-1-rulings-{l,t,n,g,c}.md. Group N first wrote its logo ruling as 2025, which Track T also used; it is 2040 (renumbered in the -n note and the titleCard.js comment).

*Group L (layout, landscape, 150% text)*
2015. The lesson row at larger text on a phone wraps (face and words first, More and Show me under); the words are never line-clamped; the chapter label is read by screen readers only until the row is open. Review S1. — Cost if wrong: the row is up to about 20 px taller at 150% and scrolls inside --guidance-max.
2016. The goals strip's words take four lines at larger text, and More gets a line of its own. Review S10. — Cost if wrong: a goal over four lines still shows More.
2017. The bar's building name uses `overflow-wrap: break-word` and the title row wraps; during a raid warning or raid, at 125% text and above, Defence leads the dock's row (`order: -1`, scroll snap off). Review S4 and S9. — Cost if wrong: the category order changes for the raid's length.
2018. `#rail` is `--rail-w` wide with the notch as its padding, counted once; each rail category keeps 8 px under its word for the colour band. Review M2 and S15. — Cost if wrong: the rail scrolls a little more at 150% sideways.
2019. The viewport meta gets `maximum-scale=2`. Keeps ruling 1829 (a deliberate pinch zooms) but caps it at 200%; the two tests that banned `maximum-scale` (v81 d noZoom, v92 a audit) accept a cap of 2 or more. Review S14. — Cost if wrong: iOS Safari ignores the cap, so an iPhone can still zoom past 200%.

*Group N (Back, sheets, the title)*
2020. The stat explainer, the Speed popup, a phone's open tray, the lesson's chapter card and the title's panels are Back steps (store fields `explainerOpen`, `speedOpen`); Back on the chapter card docks the step as Esc did. Review M1. — Cost if wrong: a stale entry makes one Back press do nothing.
2021. `shouldShowGuide` is always false: an iPhone opens on the title, and the guide opens from Install and the later ask. Its foot button says "Not now" (changes 1106's Continue for this guide only); its Export line shows only with a saved city. Review S5. — Cost if wrong: a player who wanted the guide first taps Install.
2022. A sheet opened without a named section scrolls to its top. Review S6. — Cost if wrong: none seen.
2023. The minimap sheet has the map first on a phone, and Fit the city closes the sheet before framing there. Review S7. — Cost if wrong: the alerts sit under the map; the Map badge counts them.
2024. On a phone the City page folds its sections into rows (the score's, or the named one, open); the tabs are not made sticky (review S8's sticky tabs left, see the 9.3.1 follow-ups) and the two sheet titles are unchanged. The title's decree card hides its twists, best and rival on a screen up to 720 px tall at 125% text or more. Review S8 and S13. — Cost if wrong: the twist is one tap further.
2040. (First numbered 2025.) The title's logo sits in a sticky band (`.title-head`) at the card's top; the wide card keeps it in the flow. Josh's ask. — Cost if wrong: the band takes a little height on a short screen.

*Track T (the lesson, the plots bar, the decree's buttons)*
2025. The road step's words drop the compass word: "Drag along the dashed line, from the marked end of the road." Review M3. — Cost if wrong: a player who wants a compass point loses it.
2026. The road step is framed so the marked end's middle is at least 56 px from the free map's side; while a plan is drawn the row says "Tap ✓ to build the road you drew." and the chip counts planned tiles ("9 of 9 planned"). Amends 1674 and the rule of 821 (a step's view keeps its long ring whole): at some sizes the long ring is now cut at its far end. Review M3 and S2. — Cost if wrong: the cut ring; the drag is still along the line.
2027. The housing-plots bar's "Done" becomes "Stop" (label "Stop placing homes"). Review S3. — Cost if wrong: players expecting ✕ see a word.
2028. The decree end card lists Try again (filled), Back to your city, then Share, full-width and stacked. Review S12. — Cost if wrong: Share moves from first to last.
2029. The decree chip is 44 px (`--tap`) with a chevron at its end. Review S11. — Cost if wrong: its row is 8 px taller under the top bar.

*Group G (the placing ghost's depth)*
2030. A ground layer under the sprites: `opts.drawGround(g, range)` runs after the terrain, ambient layer and ground shadows and before the sorted sprites; a ghost's footprint, washes, hatching, outlines, road joins, plots and the desirability tint draw there. — Cost if wrong: a footprint behind a tall home is hidden as the ground is; only its badge shows.
2031. Only a monument has a ghost to stand up: `opts.placing` gives the renderer its silhouette, sorted with the buildings at id -1 and drawn at 35%. — Cost if wrong: a see-through building on every ghost is new drawing work.
2032. The badges, per-square marks, glyphs, the ferry's boat and price, the bank hint, the open layer and the lesson's ring stay over everything; no faint pass of the hidden part. `drawGhost` runs in two passes (`drawGhostGround`, `drawGhostMarks`) and still draws both for demolish and the dev scenes. — Cost if wrong: a 1x1 ghost behind a tall home reads by its badge alone.
2033. Road, wall, gate and ferry previews lie on the ground too, glyphs on top; demolish keeps its whole draw over the sprites. — Cost if wrong: a road's squares behind a tall home are hidden as the built road is.
2034. At night the footprint dims with the ground (night's tint is after the sprites); badges and glyphs stay above the tint. — Cost if wrong: the outline is darker at deepest night.

*Group C (straight crossings; Josh: "It just needs to default to straight lines")*
2035. A road drag in Straight (not Route, not the Gate card) that would cross open water is laid as one straight run from where it began, along the axis it moved furthest (a tie runs along the row); a drag meeting no water keeps its L and its path. Builds on 1395. — Cost if wrong: a road that should bend after a bridge takes two strokes.
2036. The Crossing card's drag is always one straight run and lays the crossing on that line or nothing: no snap to a nearby crossing, so ruling 1347 no longer applies to the card's drag (the sim's `previewFerry` still snaps by default; `snap: false` is an option). Where it can't be laid, the bar says why and Show me goes to the nearest crossing that works. — Cost if wrong: a drag one row off a good crossing is refused, not mended.
2037. A tap with no drag still picks the nearest crossing shown (1348 kept); a new Crossing drag replaces the last line. — Cost if wrong: small.
2038. The Crossing card's crossing lands where its straight line meets dry land on each side, whatever tile the finger began or lifted on; the road tool keeps 1395's look-ahead for a stroke that stops in the water. — Cost if wrong: on the road tool, a finger a tile short of the far bank must drag on.
2039. How to play's Crossings entry says a line that meets water runs straight (`V931C_TEXT.help`) and no longer says a bent line is refused; the bar keeps its words. — Cost if wrong: none.

Rulings 2015 to 2040 are used; 2041 is the next free number.

## Part 31: Version 10.0 "A living Nile" (12 Nov 2026)

Visuals and play first, no new sound. The city is drawn alive (townsfolk, bazaar crowds, river life, builders, nights, festivals, wind, shimmer, a dust storm), the first view holds the map on screen, a wide monitor gets an Auto text size, a quality pass finishes the look, performance gets a pass, and Track M clears the phone leftovers. The sim is untouched (`git diff 0209a40 HEAD -- src/sim` is empty; `WORLD_VERSION` stays 12). Spec: specs/2026-11-11-thebes-version-10-0-a-living-nile-design.md; plan: plans/2026-11-11-thebes-version-10-0-a-living-nile.md; each track's rulings are in notes/2026-11-11-version-10-0-rulings-<track>.md. Josh's calls at the final review: the festival cue is left out (2065), the Ground legend's room key stays hidden at 375x667 and 150% (2303), a stored textSize 100 becomes Auto once (2323), the drawn storm stays and is called a "dust storm" in player words (2327), and a 160 MB desktop cache allowance rather than a cap (2295).

*The design's proposed rulings (2050 to 2065)*
2050. Confirmed, amended: the camera's rule replaces 6.0's edge rule: the map's edge stays on screen, with a margin of 0.7 x half the view, so up to 30% of the view may lie off the map (the booted start frame aims under 22%, 2320). — Cost if wrong: one constant (`holdInMap`'s margin).
2051. Confirmed: the ground past the map's edge is drawn (a rim colour paling to a haze, dunes), never a flat fill; a chunk not yet baked fills with the rim colour (2112, 2113, 2119). — Cost if wrong: a flat band at the edge.
2052. Confirmed: the scribe's bubble is a participant in the phone's guidance row, ahead of everything (2115). — Cost if wrong: a third row under the top bar.
2053. Confirmed: one filled button per surface, the rest outlined; Demolish fills only while the tool is live (2140, 2308). — Cost if wrong: one filled style swapped.
2054. Confirmed: the dock's category icons are drawn, no text glyph in the interface (2141). — Cost if wrong: an icon looks different per device.
2055. Confirmed: every sprite has a foot or contact shadow, tested by the file contract (2142, 2148). — Cost if wrong: a figure floats.
2056. Confirmed: `OWED` is empty and `EXEMPT` grew for no new slot; it was already true at `ec1d9da` (2148). — Cost if wrong: none.
2057. Confirmed: life is render-side only, a pure function of world, time and a hash; nothing in `src/render` or `src/ui` writes the world (the review checked the diff). The spec's id ranges for life (5a to 5d, 1600000 and up) are superseded by 2101. — Cost if wrong: a render write could desync a save.
2058. Confirmed: the LIFE budget is 1.5 ms on a desktop and 1.0 ms on a phone with its own dropper (`LIFE.budgetMs`, `LIFE.dropOrder`; 2102); measured at 0.07 to 0.73 ms (2251) and 0.46 to 1.19 ms in the paired night runs (2296). — Cost if wrong: life eats a frame; the dropper sheds it.
2059. Confirmed: Full, Gentle and Still mean what section 5 says for every new feature (2200 to 2254). — Cost if wrong: a motion setting moves what it should not.
2060. Confirmed: one wind drifts and everything that leans reads it (`windAt`, 2230). — Cost if wrong: reeds and smoke lean apart.
2061. Confirmed, amended: storms are seeded by map number and year, never in the first year or the lesson, and touch no rule; 1 or 2 a year from the second year, 20 to 40 s (2232). In player words it is a "dust storm" that changes nothing, because the sim already has a "sandstorm" event that spoils stores (Josh, decision 4; 2327). — Cost if wrong: a player expects the event's losses.
2062. Confirmed, amended: the budget tests' bounds are the 9.1 targets plus headroom, held unthrottled (2105, 2294, the harness cannot throttle); at a 4x throttle the phone stress city is still about 3.3 to 4.5 times over its target (2292, notes/2026-11-12-version-10-0-followups.md). — Cost if wrong: a real phone drops frames the suite cannot see.
2063. Confirmed: the default UI scale follows the width on a fine pointer, a stored choice wins (2120 to 2127); a stored 9.x 100 counts as Auto once (2323). — Cost if wrong: a player lands on a size they did not pick.
2064. Confirmed: no feature loosens a bound; two records are honest changes: the phone stress median is the in-suite measure x 1.1 (2294) and the night frame is judged as life's added cost over the light alone (2296); no day bound moved. — Cost if wrong: a slow frame passes.
2065. Amended: no new sound in 10.0. The festival piece and its cue do not exist in the code, a festival piece is new music, and 10.0 rules new music out (2243; Josh, decision 1). — Cost if wrong: festivals are silent, as they were.

*Track 0 (the seams, 2100 to 2105)*
2100. `zoomAt` holds by the view; `panBy` holds only when given one (until Track F passed the view to the pans).
2101. Life's ids are 2000000 to 2799999 (the plan's 1600000 range fell inside wildlife's); `walkSlotFor` takes the renderer's object.
2102. The life seam keeps the v90 dropper; a frame's hooks add up under one `now()`, and the dropping test runs 200 frames.
2103. The life scenes live in `src/dev/v100/index.js`; `tools/shots.ps1 -V100 -Track` names the track.
2104. The phone frame test has no `it.skip` and no log; its bounds sit behind `BOUNDS_ON` and its numbers print as a test name.
2105. Today's numbers (11 Nov): the in-suite budget tests measure unthrottled (Josh approved); the 4x-throttle table is the follow-ups' baseline.

*Track F (the first view, 2110 to 2121)*
2110. Every camera write holds (`jumpTo`, tweens, the fling, the drags and the keyboard pan pass the view).
2111. `startCamera(world, view, zoom = 1)` runs after `createUI`, for a fresh city only; the phone's opening zoom is kept.
2112. `TERRAIN.offMap` stays `#C8AE7C`; the ground is that rim colour paling to a haze 12 tiles out.
2113. `offMap.js` is built to cost (no `fillPattern`, one canvas, one bake), since 12 full fills cost 16 to 30 ms in software canvas.
2114. The rim shadow is one tile outside the rim, over the existing `drawMapEdge` band.
2115. The scribe is a participant in the guidance row (`data-guidance="scribe"`), a pinned line floats and takes none.
2116. Shots: `fresh-corner` takes its size from the page's `?size=`; before and after were taken on 48, 64, 80, 112 and the default.
2117. `tests/v50/layers/ui.test.js` calls `hush()` before reading the Town tip, since the scribe now holds the row first.
2118. The lesson's framing tolerance is 80 px, not 2, because the map's edge on screen outranks exact centring.
2119. Fix rounds: the start nudges up to 2 m under 27%, the off-map ground bakes view plus margin and pans by blit, a missing chunk fills instead of smearing, and a resize re-holds.
2320. Fix wave (F-1): `startCamera` aims under 0.22, and the booted test holds three seeds at each size.
2321. Fix wave (F-2): the scribe test polls for the fade (2 s cap) instead of waiting 520 ms.

*Track D (the desktop scale, 2120 to 2129)*
2120. Auto is `textSize: null`; `settings.get().textSize` is the size in use (125% from 1600 px, 150% from 2200 px, fine pointer only).
2121. A missing resize listener is added, so Auto follows the window.
2122. `body[data-layout="wide"]` scales the panel, drawer, minimap and title card; phone layouts keep 320 and 268.
2123. The panel's minimap is the same 160 px canvas sized by CSS (a little soft at 125% and 150%).
2124. The sheet's inset on the wide layout is the panel's measured width.
2125. The wide bar's long date is `Y2 · M9 · D1` (`compactDateText`); the name and aria-label keep "Year 2, month 9, day 1".
2126. `tests/ui/settings.test.js` and `menu.test.js` compare with `textSize: 100` (Auto with no window).
2127. The phone frame sizes cannot change: Auto needs 1600 px and a fine pointer.
2322. Final review D-1: the wide bar's numbers scale in full (`--stat-num: 15px x scale`), 18 px or more at 1920 Auto.
2323. Final review D-2 (Josh yes): a stored 9.x `textSize: 100` with no marker becomes Auto once; `textSizeAutoOffered` marks it and a later pick of 100 is kept.
2324. Final review D-3: the window env is read once and again only when it changes.

*Track Q (the quality pass, 2140 to 2148)*
2140. One button language: the tray's Demolish is outlined coral and fills only while pressed; the building sheet's Demolish is outlined.
2141. Drawn category icons (`CATEGORY_ICON_DRAWS`, `categoryIconUrl`), cached by id, size, colour and ratio.
2142. Foot shadows on the raider chariot, elephant and boat raider, the ibis and heron; `waterShadow` under the hippo, fishing boat and wharf.
2143. Plots are pegged out in the ground with stakes and a cord, baked into the chunk.
2144. Paved streets get wheel tracks (`WEAR`), baked in `terrainRoads.js`.
2145. Burnt ground shows charred timbers and an ash drift; problem markers draw 20% smaller (`MARKER.drawScale` 0.8, tap area unchanged).
2146. Legibility is tested as ring and pill 3:1 and road and water 25, since every word is drawn after the light pass; no colours changed.
2147. The title's backdrop is a Capital with a festival held 12 days ago (`capitalBackdrop`, a fixture that never ticks).
2148. The drawings 9.3 did not reach were mostly reached already; a short pass finished the rest.

*Track L (life and motion, 2200 to 2254, and the fix wave 2326 to 2329)*
2200. Poses are kinds of their own, as `soldier_cheer` is a soldier's.
2201. The hour comes from a peek at the light loop, not from `lightClock`.
2202. The share of homes with a townsperson moves smoothly with the hour.
2203. The cap is met from a fixed set of homes: the nearest 1.6 x cap to the view's middle.
2204. The bank's figures are `woman_kneel` and the water carrier, next to open water within 2 tiles.
2205. The walker entry gains `alpha`, `lift` and `depth`.
2206. The crowd is 3 + 5 x busy(hour), at most 3 bazaars nearest the middle, the last figure fading.
2207. "Short of food" is under 10 food on the shelf: one figure.
2208. The lesson's crowd waits on the `workers` step (the plan's food step does not exist), read through `stepDone`.
2209. The `bazaar-crowd` scene is set in the morning, the busiest hour.
2210. No second wharf boat: the River track already sends one out; `fisher_cast` waits (placed by 2329).
2211. Feluccas use the trade boat's slots and cross a river row near the middle, from City (two at Capital).
2212. A heron lifts in two frames by the nearest boat's distance and is gone under 0.9 tile until it has passed.

## Part 32: Version 10.0.1 "Your feedback" (13 Nov 2026)

A patch from Josh's nine play notes: the desktop tip card, the Rushed label, keys shown and rebindable, defenders that go anywhere, faster and steadier raiders, livelier animals, storehouses that fetch for their neighbours, and a bottom bar that stays put. The sim changed only where Track S ruled (raid pace, who goes out, the storehouse fetch); `WORLD_VERSION` stays 12. The version saves two optional fields, `raid.active.stride` and `unit.chase` (ruling 2394), both in free records and absent in 10.0 saves. Plan: plans/2026-11-12-thebes-version-10-0-1-feedback.md; each track's rulings are in notes/2026-11-12-version-10-0-1-rulings-<track>.md. Josh's calls at the final review: D1 = B (a unit goes out at once to any band within its 10.0 reach of home, 8, 10 and 12 tiles, and beyond that to any band that threatens the city; ruling 2420) and D2 = yes (ship with the long-run balance misses and carry them to 10.1 "A fair reign"). What is left is in notes/2026-11-12-version-10-0-1-followups.md.
